from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons

print("Querying CCLE.csv for YES1 with classification ecDNA or BFB...")
result = query_amplicons(gene=['YES1'], classification=['ecDNA', 'BFB'], csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print basic info about the result
print("Saved result to variable 'result'. Now inspecting structure...")
print("Type of result:", type(result))

# If dict-like, show keys and number of rows, and print each row concisely
try:
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        print("Top-level keys:", list(result.keys()))
        rows = result.get('rows') or result.get('data') or result.get('rows', [])
        if rows is None:
            rows = []
        print("Number of matching rows:", len(rows))
        for i, row in enumerate(rows):
            print(f"\n--- Row {i+1} ---")
            # Print selected fields for readability
            keys_of_interest = ['Sample name', 'sample_name', 'Feature ID', 'feature_id', 'Classification', 'classification', 'Oncogenes', 'oncogenes', 'All genes', 'all_genes', 'Feature maximum copy number', 'feature_max_copy_number']
            for k in keys_of_interest:
                if k in row:
                    print(f"{k}: {row[k]}")
            # If normalized columns exist, print them too
            if 'sample_name' in row and 'classification' in row:
                print("Record (sample_name, classification):", row.get('sample_name'), row.get('classification'))
    else:
        print(result)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error inspecting result:", e)

# Save result for later use
query_result = result
print("\nQuery finished. Variable 'query_result' holds the returned object.")
</execute>

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons

print("Querying CCLE.csv for YES1 with classification ecDNA or BFB and selecting detailed columns...")

detailed_result = query_amplicons(
    gene=['YES1'],
    classification=['ecDNA', 'BFB'],
    csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
    select=['Sample name','Feature ID','Classification','Location','Oncogenes','All genes','Complexity score','Captured interval length','Feature median copy number','Feature maximum copy number','Tissue of origin'],
    add_normalized_columns=True
)

print("Saved result to variable 'detailed_result'. Inspecting returned object...")

print("Top-level keys:", list(detailed_result.keys()))
print("Total matching rows returned:", detailed_result.get('row_count_returned'))

rows = detailed_result.get('rows', [])
for i, row in enumerate(rows):
    print(f"\n--- Match {i+1} ---")
    # Print all selected fields if present
    fields = ['Sample name','sample_name','Feature ID','feature_id','Classification','classification','Location','location','Oncogenes','oncogenes','All genes','all_genes','Complexity score','complexity_score','Captured interval length','captured_interval_length','Feature median copy number','feature_median_copy_number','Feature maximum copy number','feature_max_copy_number','Tissue of origin','tissue_of_origin']
    for f in fields:
        if f in row:
            print(f"{f}: {row[f]}")
    # Also print a concise summary line
    sample = row.get('sample_name') or row.get('Sample name')
    classification = row.get('classification') or row.get('Classification')
    max_cn = row.get('feature_max_copy_number') or row.get('Feature maximum copy number')
    print(f"Summary: sample={sample}, classification={classification}, max_copy_number={max_cn}")

print("\nQuery complete. Variable 'detailed_result' contains the detailed matches.")
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("Step 1: Querying amplicons with classification = 'BFB' ...")
# Query BFB amplicons, request normalized columns to make column names predictable
result = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print the raw result (requirement: save output and print)
print("\nRaw query result type:", type(result))
try:
    # If result is a dict-like object, print keys to inspect structure
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        print("Result keys():", list(result.keys()))
    # Print a short preview
    print("\nPreview of raw result (first 3 items if available):")
    if hasattr(result, 'head'):
        print(result.head(3))
    else:
        # try printing first few rows if it's dict with 'rows' or 'data'
        if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
            import itertools, json
            print(json.dumps(result['rows'][:3], indent=2) if len(result['rows'])>0 else result['rows'])
        else:
            print(result)
except Exception as e:
    print("Could not pretty-print raw result:", e)

# Try to convert to a DataFrame for analysis

    df = result.copy()
elif isinstance(result, dict):
    # Try common keys
    if 'rows' in result and isinstance(result['rows'], list):
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
    elif 'data' in result and isinstance(result['data'], list):
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['data'])
    elif 'results' in result and isinstance(result['results'], list):
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['results'])
    else:
        # fallback: try to find any list-valued key
        for k,v in result.items():
            if isinstance(v, list):
                df = pd.DataFrame(v)
                print(f"Converted list under key '{k}' to DataFrame for inspection.")
                break

if df is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Unable to convert query result to a DataFrame for analysis. Result printed above for inspection.")

print("\nStep 2: DataFrame columns:")
print(df.columns.tolist())

# Identify captured interval length column by trying likely normalized names
candidates = [
    'captured_interval_length', 
    'Captured interval length', 
    'Captured Interval Length', 
    'captured interval length', 
    'captured_interval_length_bp',
    'captured_interval_length_bp',
    'capturedIntervalLength'
]
col_name = None
for c in candidates:
    if c in df.columns:
        col_name = c
        break
# Also consider snake-case normalized mapping if available
if col_name is None:
    # try column names lowercased and stripped
    lower_map = {col.lower().replace(" ", "_").replace("-", "_"): col for col in df.columns}
    if 'captured_interval_length' in lower_map:
        col_name = lower_map['captured_interval_length']

if col_name is None:
    # print a short sample of columns that contain 'capt' to help find name
    capt_cols = [col for col in df.columns if 'capt' in col.lower()]
    print("\nColumns containing 'capt':", capt_cols)
    raise RuntimeError("Could not find a captured-interval-length column automatically. See columns above.")

print(f"\nUsing column for captured interval length: '{col_name}'")

# Convert column to numeric (remove NAs)
series = pd.to_numeric(df[col_name], errors='coerce')
series_nonan = series.dropna().astype(float)

n_total = len(series)
n_nonan = len(series_nonan)
print(f"\nNumber of BFB amplicon records returned: {len(df)}")
print(f"Non-missing captured-interval-length values: {n_nonan} (missing: {n_total-n_nonan})")

# Step 3: summary statistics
stats = {
    'count': int(n_nonan),
    'min_bp': float(series_nonan.min()) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    '25pct_bp': float(series_nonan.quantile(0.25)) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    'median_bp': float(series_nonan.median()) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    'mean_bp': float(series_nonan.mean()) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    '75pct_bp': float(series_nonan.quantile(0.75)) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    'max_bp': float(series_nonan.max()) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    'std_bp': float(series_nonan.std(ddof=1)) if n_nonan>1 else None,
    '90pct_bp': float(series_nonan.quantile(0.90)) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    '95pct_bp': float(series_nonan.quantile(0.95)) if n_nonan>0 else None,
    'iqr_bp': float(series_nonan.quantile(0.75) - series_nonan.quantile(0.25)) if n_nonan>0 else None
}
print("\nStep 3: Summary statistics (bp):")
for k,v in stats.items():
    print(f"  {k}: {v}")

# Step 4: binned distribution with bins in bp
bins = [0, 1_000, 10_000, 100_000, 1_000_000, 10_000_000, np.inf]
bin_labels = ['<=1kb', '1kb-10kb', '10kb-100kb', '100kb-1Mb', '1Mb-10Mb', '>10Mb']
binned = pd.cut(series_nonan, bins=bins, labels=bin_labels, right=True, include_lowest=True)
bin_counts = binned.value_counts().reindex(bin_labels).fillna(0).astype(int)

print("\nStep 4: Binned size distribution (counts and percentages):")
for label in bin_labels:
    cnt = int(bin_counts[label])
    pct = (cnt / n_nonan * 100) if n_nonan>0 else 0
    print(f"  {label}: {cnt} ({pct:.1f}%)")

# Also print a few extreme examples (smallest 5, largest 5)
print("\nSmallest 5 captured-interval-length values (bp):")
print(series_nonan.nsmallest(5).tolist())
print("\nLargest 5 captured-interval-length values (bp):")
print(series_nonan.nlargest(5).tolist())

# Save outputs for presentation
analysis = {
    'column_used': col_name,
    'n_records': len(df),
    'n_nonmissing': n_nonan,
    'stats': stats,
    'bin_counts': bin_counts.to_dict(),
    'smallest5': series_nonan.nsmallest(5).tolist(),
    'largest5': series_nonan.nlargest(5).tolist()
}

print("\nAnalysis object prepared.")
# Print the analysis dict as final printed output
print("\nFinal analysis summary object:")
import json
print(json.dumps({
    'column_used': analysis['column_used'],
    'n_records': analysis['n_records'],
    'n_nonmissing': analysis['n_nonmissing'],
    'stats': {k:(v if v is None else (round(v,2) if isinstance(v,(int,float)) else v)) for k,v in analysis['stats'].items()},
    'bin_counts': analysis['bin_counts']
}, indent=2))

# Save variable 'analysis' for potential further use
analysis_result = analysis
print("\nSaved 'analysis_result'.")

result = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

print("Type of result:", type(result))

# Save and print the results.

# Access the bfb_records variable saved in the previous step
try:
    bfb = bfb_records
    print("bfb_records found in environment.")
except NameError:
    # If not present, reload BFB records from the CCLE.csv
    from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
    print("bfb_records not found; re-querying CCLE.csv for BFB classification...")
    bfb = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print schema_normalized keys for reference
schema_norm = bfb.get('schema_normalized', None)
print("\nSchema normalized (keys preview):")
if schema_norm:
    keys = list(schema_norm.keys())
    print(keys[:50])
else:
    print("No schema_normalized present.")

rows = bfb.get('rows', [])
print(f"\nTotal BFB rows loaded: {len(rows)}")

# Filter for sample_name containing 'HARA' (case-insensitive)
matches = [r for r in rows if 'sample_name' in r and 'HARA' in str(r['sample_name']).upper()]

print(f"Number of BFB amplicons in samples containing 'HARA': {len(matches)}\n")

# Prepare and print formatted results
results = []
for r in matches:
    sample = r.get('Sample name', r.get('sample_name'))
    aa_num = r.get('AA amplicon number', r.get('aa_amplicon_number'))
    # Format feature id
    try:
        aa_int = int(aa_num) if aa_num is not None else "NA"
    except:
        aa_int = aa_num
    feature_id = f"{sample}_AA{aa_int}"
    location = r.get('Location', r.get('location'))
    oncogenes = r.get('Oncogenes', r.get('oncogenes'))
    all_genes = r.get('All genes', r.get('all_genes'))
    feature_max_cn = r.get('Feature maximum copy number', r.get('feature_maximum_copy_number'))
    feature_median_cn = r.get('Feature median copy number', r.get('feature_median_copy_number'))
    entry = {
        'feature_id': feature_id,
        'sample_name': sample,
        'aa_amplicon_number': aa_num,
        'location': location,
        'oncogenes': oncogenes,
        'all_genes': all_genes,
        'feature_max_copy_number': feature_max_cn,
        'feature_median_copy_number': feature_median_cn
    }
    results.append(entry)

# Save output and print
matched_results = results
print("Matched entries (formatted):")
for i, e in enumerate(matched_results, 1):
    print(f"\nEntry {i}:")
    print(" Feature ID:", e['feature_id'])
    print(" Sample name:", e['sample_name'])
    print(" AA amplicon number:", e['aa_amplicon_number'])
    print(" Location:", e['location'])
    print(" Oncogenes:", e['oncogenes'])
    print(" All genes:", e['all_genes'])
    print(" Feature max copy number:", e['feature_max_copy_number'])
    print(" Feature median copy number:", e['feature_median_copy_number'])

print(f"\nTotal matches returned: {len(matched_results)}")

matched_bfb_hara = matched_results

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons

# Ensure we have the BFB records; if not, query again
try:
    bfb = bfb_records  # from previous cell
    print("Using existing 'bfb_records' variable.")
except NameError:
    print("'bfb_records' not found in environment; querying CCLE.csv for BFB classification...")
    bfb = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Determine rows container
if isinstance(bfb, dict) and 'rows' in bfb:
    rows = bfb['rows']
elif isinstance(bfb, list):
    rows = bfb
else:
    # fallback: try common keys
    rows = bfb.get('rows', [])
    
print(f"Total rows available for BFB: {len(rows)}")

# Inspect column keys from the first row to identify correct field names
if len(rows) > 0:
    sample_keys = list(rows[0].keys())
    print("Example row keys (first row):", sample_keys)
else:
    print("No rows to inspect.")

# Filter for sample name containing 'HARA' (case-insensitive) using either 'sample_name' or 'Sample name'
matches = []
for r in rows:
    # attempt to get sample name from normalized or original key
    sample = None
    if 'sample_name' in r and r['sample_name'] is not None:
        sample = str(r['sample_name'])
    elif 'Sample name' in r and r['Sample name'] is not None:
        sample = str(r['Sample name'])
    elif 'Sample' in r and r['Sample'] is not None:
        sample = str(r['Sample'])
    # skip if no sample name
    if not sample:
        continue
    if 'HARA' in sample.upper():
        matches.append(r)

print(f"Number of matching BFB amplicons for samples containing 'HARA': {len(matches)}")

# Format matches into requested fields
formatted = []
for r in matches:
    sample = r.get('Sample name') or r.get('sample_name') or ""
    aa_num = r.get('AA amplicon number') or r.get('aa_amplicon_number') or ""
    feature_id = f"{sample}_AA{int(aa_num) if isinstance(aa_num, (int, float)) and not pd.isna(aa_num) else aa_num}"
    location = r.get('Location') or r.get('location') or ""
    oncogenes = r.get('Oncogenes') or r.get('oncogenes') or ""
    all_genes = r.get('All genes') or r.get('all_genes') or ""
    feature_max_cn = r.get('Feature maximum copy number') or r.get('feature_maximum_copy_number') or ""
    feature_median_cn = r.get('Feature median copy number') or r.get('feature_median_copy_number') or ""
    formatted.append({
        'feature_id': feature_id,
        'sample_name': sample,
        'aa_amplicon_number': aa_num,
        'location': location,
        'oncogenes': oncogenes,
        'all_genes': all_genes,
        'feature_max_copy_number': feature_max_cn,
        'feature_median_copy_number': feature_median_cn
    })

# Print formatted results
if len(formatted) == 0:
    print("No BFB amplicons found for HARA.")
else:
    for i, e in enumerate(formatted, 1):
        print(f"\nMatch {i}:")
        print(" Feature ID:", e['feature_id'])
        print(" Sample name:", e['sample_name'])
        print(" AA amplicon number:", e['aa_amplicon_number'])
        print(" Location:", e['location'])
        print(" Oncogenes:", e['oncogenes'])
        print(" All genes:", e['all_genes'])
        print(" Feature max copy number:", e['feature_max_copy_number'])
        print(" Feature median copy number:", e['feature_median_copy_number'])

# Save to variable for user reference
matched_bfb_hara = formatted
print("\nSaved results to 'matched_bfb_hara'.")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import re
from collections import Counter

print("1) Querying amplicons with classification='BFB' from CCLE.csv ...")
result = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print the raw result summary as required
print("\n--- RAW QUERY RESULT TYPE AND SUMMARY ---")
print("Type:", type(result))

# Try to inspect typical structure: if it's a dict with keys or a pandas-like object
try:
    keys = result.keys()
    print("Top-level keys:", list(keys)[:50])
except Exception as e:
    print("Could not list keys (exception):", e)

# If result looks like a list of dicts
rows = None
if isinstance(result, dict):
    # Try typical fields used by this tool
    if 'rows' in result and isinstance(result['rows'], list):
        rows = result['rows']
        print("Rows found in result['rows'], number of rows:", len(rows))
    else:
        # maybe the dict itself is a single record or mapping; try to interpret as list
        # attempt to find a list-valued key that looks like rows
        list_keys = [k for k,v in result.items() if isinstance(v, list)]
        if list_keys:
            rows = result[list_keys[0]]
            print(f"Using first list-valued key '{list_keys[0]}' with {len(rows)} entries as rows.")
        else:
            # fallback: wrap dict as single row
            rows = [result]
            print("No list-valued key found; treating dict as single row.")
elif isinstance(result, list):
    rows = result
    print("Result is a list with length:", len(rows))
else:
    # Try treating as pandas-like (DataFrame)
    try:
        import pandas as pd
        if hasattr(result, 'head'):
            df = result
            rows = df.to_dict(orient='records')
            print("Result appears DataFrame-like; converted to list of records; rows:", len(rows))
        else:
            # fallback: string representation
            print("Unknown result structure; printing repr:")
            print(repr(result)[:1000])
            rows = []
    except Exception as e:
        print("Could not interpret result as DataFrame:", e)
        rows = []

# Print sample of first up to 3 rows for inspection
print("\n--- SAMPLE ROWS (up to 3) ---")
for i, r in enumerate(rows[:3]):
    print(f"ROW {i+1}:")
    # Try pretty print fields
    if isinstance(r, dict):
        # print keys and a few values
        for k in list(r.keys())[:20]:
            val = r.get(k)
            print(f"  {k}: {str(val)[:200]}")
    else:
        print(str(r)[:1000])

# Step: Parse genes and count occurrences across amplicons
print("\n2) Parsing gene fields and counting frequencies...")

# Determine candidate gene columns from dictionary keys if available
candidate_gene_cols = set()
if rows:
    # gather keys from first few rows
    sample_keys = set()
    for r in rows[:10]:
        if isinstance(r, dict):
            sample_keys.update(r.keys())
    # Potential gene-related columns (common names)
    for k in sample_keys:
        kl = k.lower()
        if 'gene' in kl:
            candidate_gene_cols.add(k)
    # fallback common names
    fallback_cols = ['all_genes','All_genes','oncogenes','Oncogenes','genes','Genes','genes_in_amplicon','genes_list']
    for fc in fallback_cols:
        if fc in sample_keys:
            candidate_gene_cols.add(fc)

print("Candidate gene columns detected:", candidate_gene_cols)

# Function to extract gene list from a row using available columns
def extract_genes_from_row(row, candidate_cols):
    genes = set()
    if not isinstance(row, dict):
        return genes
    for col in candidate_cols:
        if col in row and row[col] and isinstance(row[col], str):
            s = row[col]
            # some entries might be semicolon/comma/pipe separated; split on common delimiters
            parts = re.split(r'[;,\|/]+', s)
            for p in parts:
                gp = p.strip()
                if gp and gp.upper() not in ('NA','NONE','N/A'):
                    genes.add(gp)
    return genes

# Count genes across rows (each amplicon contributes at most 1 count per gene)
gene_counter = Counter()
amplicon_count = 0
for r in rows:
    if not isinstance(r, dict):
        continue
    # Extract genes
    genes = extract_genes_from_row(r, candidate_gene_cols)
    if genes:
        amplicon_count += 1
        for g in genes:
            gene_counter[g] += 1

print(f"Number of BFB amplicons inspected (with gene info present): {amplicon_count}")
print("Total distinct genes found in these amplicons:", len(gene_counter))

# Get top 25 genes
top25 = gene_counter.most_common(25)

print("\n--- Top 25 genes most frequently appearing in BFB amplicons ---")
for rank, (gene, cnt) in enumerate(top25, start=1):
    print(f"{rank:2d}. {gene} — {cnt}")

# Save outputs as required
result_summary = {
    'amplicons_with_genes': amplicon_count,
    'distinct_genes_found': len(gene_counter),
    'top25': top25,
    'candidate_gene_columns': list(candidate_gene_cols)
}

print("\n--- RESULT SUMMARY DICT ---")
print(result_summary)

_query_result = result
_query_processing_summary = result_summary

print("\nNote: Each BFB amplicon record was searched for gene lists in detected gene-related columns (e.g., 'all_genes' / 'oncogenes').")
print("For each amplicon, unique gene symbols present were counted once per amplicon. Frequencies reflect the number of BFB amplicons that include each gene.")

<execute>
# Step 1: Re-query and robustly parse gene lists to produce a clean top 25
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
import re
from collections import Counter

print("Querying CCLE for BFB amplicons...")
res = query_amplicons(classification='BFB', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Query returned top-level keys:", list(res.keys()) if isinstance(res, dict) else type(res))

rows = res.get('rows', []) if isinstance(res, dict) else (res if isinstance(res, list) else [])
print(f"Number of BFB amplicon records returned: {len(rows)}")

# Determine relevant columns
possible_cols = ['all_genes', 'All genes', 'oncogenes', 'Oncogenes']
present_cols = [c for c in possible_cols if any(isinstance(r, dict) and c in r for r in rows)]
print("Gene-related columns present:", present_cols)

def parse_gene_field(val):
    """
    Robust parsing of gene field values observed in the dataset.
    Handles:
      - strings representing Python lists: "['GENE1', 'GENE2']"
      - strings with brackets and quoted items: '["\'GENE1\'", "\'GENE2\'"]'
      - comma/semicolon/pipe-separated strings
      - single gene strings
    Returns a list of cleaned gene symbols.
    """
    genes = []
    if val is None:
        return genes
    if isinstance(val, (list, tuple)):
        items = list(val)
    else:
        s = str(val).strip()
        # Try to literal_eval if it looks like a list
        if s.startswith('[') and s.endswith(']'):
            try:
                items = ast.literal_eval(s)
            except Exception:
                # fallback: strip outer brackets and split by comma
                inner = s[1:-1]
                items = re.split(r',\s*', inner) if inner else []
        else:
            # split on common delimiters
            items = re.split(r'[;,\|/]+', s)
    # clean each item
    for it in items:
        if it is None:
            continue
        t = str(it).strip()
        # remove extra surrounding quotes/apostrophes and whitespace
        t = t.strip(' "\'[]')
        # skip empty or NA-like tokens
        if not t or t.upper() in ('NA','N/A','NONE'):
            continue
        genes.append(t)
    return genes

# Iterate rows and collect genes per amplicon (unique per amplicon)
gene_counter = Counter()
amp_with_genes = 0
amp_missing_genes = 0

for r in rows:
    if not isinstance(r, dict):
        continue
    genes_set = set()
    for col in present_cols:
        if col in r and r[col]:
            parsed = parse_gene_field(r[col])
            for g in parsed:
                genes_set.add(g)
    if genes_set:
        amp_with_genes += 1
        for g in genes_set:
            gene_counter[g] += 1
    else:
        amp_missing_genes += 1

print(f"BFB amplicons with >=1 gene annotation: {amp_with_genes}")
print(f"BFB amplicons with no gene annotation: {amp_missing_genes}")
print(f"Distinct genes found across BFB amplicons: {len(gene_counter)}")

top25 = gene_counter.most_common(50)  # get more so we can exclude blanks if needed

# If an empty-string token was counted, report and exclude it
if '' in gene_counter:
    print("Found empty-string gene token with count:", gene_counter[''])
    # remove it
    del gene_counter['']
    top25 = gene_counter.most_common(25)
else:
    top25 = gene_counter.most_common(25)

print("\nTop 25 genes (gene, count) — cleaned:")
for i, (g,c) in enumerate(top25, start=1):
    print(f"{i:2d}. {g} — {c}")

# Save outputs to variables for inspection
_clean_top25 = top25
_summary = {
    'total_BFB_amplicons_returned': len(rows),
    'amplicons_with_genes': amp_with_genes,
    'amplicons_missing_genes': amp_missing_genes,
    'distinct_genes': len(gene_counter),
    'top25': top25,
    'gene_columns_used': present_cols
}

print("\nSummary:", _summary)
</execute>

import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Step 2: Load amplicon data from CCLE
print("\nStep 2: Querying amplicon data (CCLE)...")
csv_path = "./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv"
result = query_amplicons(csv_path=csv_path)  # save output as required
print("Result type:", type(result))

# Try to coerce to a DataFrame depending on result type
if isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result
elif isinstance(result, dict):
    # print keys to inspect
    print("Result keys:", list(result.keys()))
    # common patterns: 'rows' or 'data' or 'records'
    if 'rows' in result:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
    elif 'data' in result:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['data'])
    elif 'records' in result:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['records'])
    else:
        # fallback: attempt to create DataFrame directly
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(result)
        except Exception as e:
            raise RuntimeError("Unable to convert query result to DataFrame: " + str(e))
elif isinstance(result, list):
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)
else:
    # fallback conversion
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)
print("Data loaded into DataFrame with shape:", df.shape)

# Step 3: Inspect columns and sample rows
print("\nStep 3: Inspecting columns and sample rows...")
print("Columns:", list(df.columns)[:50])
print("\nSample rows (first 5):")
print(df.head(5).to_string(index=False))

# Identify likely column names for tissue and classification
cols_lower = [c.lower() for c in df.columns]
tissue_col = None
class_col = None
# try to match common names
for c in df.columns:
    cl = c.lower()
    if 'tissue' in cl and 'origin' in cl:
        tissue_col = c
    if cl == 'tissue of origin':
        tissue_col = c
    if 'classification' in cl or cl == 'classification':
        class_col = c
# fallback tries
if tissue_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'tissue' in c.lower():
            tissue_col = c
            break
if class_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'class' in c.lower():
            class_col = c
            break

print("\nDetected tissue column:", tissue_col)
print("Detected classification column:", class_col)
if tissue_col is None or class_col is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Could not identify Tissue and/or Classification columns automatically. Columns found: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

# Clean possible NA entries
df = df[[tissue_col, class_col]].copy()
df = df.dropna(subset=[tissue_col, class_col])
df[tissue_col] = df[tissue_col].astype(str).str.strip()
df[class_col] = df[class_col].astype(str).str.strip()

# Step 4: Compute distribution counts and percentages
print("\nStep 4: Computing distribution counts and percentages...")
counts = df.groupby([tissue_col, class_col]).size().unstack(fill_value=0)
# sort tissues by total count descending
counts['__total__'] = counts.sum(axis=1)
counts = counts.sort_values('__total__', ascending=False)
total_col = counts.pop('__total__')
counts['__total__'] = total_col

# percentages per tissue
percents = counts.drop(columns='__total__').div(counts['__total__'], axis=0) * 100

print("\nCounts table (top 10 tissues):")
print(counts.head(10).to_string())

print("\nPercent table (top 10 tissues):")
print(percents.head(10).round(2).to_string())

# Step 5: Plotting and saving results
print("\nStep 5: Creating and saving plots and CSV...")

out_dir = "amplicon_class_by_tissue_outputs"
os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)

# Percent stacked bar plot
plt.figure(figsize=(14,8))
percents_plot = percents.copy()
# If too many tissues, limit to top 25 for visibility
top_n = 25
if percents_plot.shape[0] > top_n:
    percents_plot = percents_plot.iloc[:top_n]
ax = percents_plot.plot(kind='bar', stacked=True, colormap='tab20', width=0.8)
plt.ylabel("Percent of amplicons (%)")
plt.xlabel("Tissue of origin")
plt.title("Distribution (percentage) of amplification classes across top tissues")
plt.legend(title="Classification", bbox_to_anchor=(1.02, 1), loc='upper left')
plt.tight_layout()
percent_plot_path = os.path.join(out_dir, "amplicon_class_distribution_percent_by_tissue_top25.png")
plt.savefig(percent_plot_path, dpi=150)
plt.close()

# Counts stacked bar plot
plt.figure(figsize=(14,8))
counts_plot = counts.drop(columns='__total__').copy()
if counts_plot.shape[0] > top_n:
    counts_plot = counts_plot.iloc[:top_n]
counts_plot.plot(kind='bar', stacked=True, colormap='tab20', width=0.8)
plt.ylabel("Number of amplicons")
plt.xlabel("Tissue of origin")
plt.title("Distribution (counts) of amplification classes across top tissues")
plt.legend(title="Classification", bbox_to_anchor=(1.02, 1), loc='upper left')
plt.tight_layout()
count_plot_path = os.path.join(out_dir, "amplicon_class_distribution_counts_by_tissue_top25.png")
plt.savefig(count_plot_path, dpi=150)
plt.close()

# Save CSV table of counts and percentages
counts_csv = os.path.join(out_dir, "amplicon_class_counts_by_tissue.csv")
percents_csv = os.path.join(out_dir, "amplicon_class_percents_by_tissue.csv")
counts.to_csv(counts_csv)
percents.to_csv(percents_csv)

print("Saved percent stacked bar plot to:", percent_plot_path)
print("Saved count stacked bar plot to:", count_plot_path)
print("Saved counts CSV to:", counts_csv)
print("Saved percents CSV to:", percents_csv)

# Step 6: finalize and show top-level summary
print("\nStep 6: Summary of top tissues by total amplicons:")
summary = counts[['__total__']].reset_index().rename(columns={tissue_col:'Tissue', '__total__':'Total_amplicons'})
print(summary.head(15).to_string(index=False))

print("\nResearch log: Completed")

# Save variables to inspect if needed
results_summary = {
    "dataframe_shape": df.shape,
    "tissue_col": tissue_col,
    "class_col": class_col,
    "counts_head": counts.head(10),
    "percents_head": percents.head(10),
    "saved_files": {
        "percent_plot": percent_plot_path,
        "count_plot": count_plot_path,
        "counts_csv": counts_csv,
        "percents_csv": percents_csv
    }
}

# Print concise object so environment can capture it

print(results_summary)

print("\nResearch log: Step 2 - Querying the full dataset (selecting sample_name, tissue_of_origin, classification).")
res = query_amplicons(select=['sample_name','tissue_of_origin','classification'], csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Top-level keys in full query result:", list(res.keys()))

print("Constructing DataFrame from rows...")
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)

# Use normalized column names if available
cols = df.columns
# Determine which normalized columns exist
for c in ['sample_name','tissue_of_origin','classification']:
    if c not in cols:
        raise ValueError(f"Expected column '{c}' not found in returned rows. Available cols: {cols[:30]}")

print("DataFrame shape (rows, cols):", df.shape)
print("Example rows:")
print(df.head(8))

print("\nResearch log: Step 3 - Aggregate per sample to determine ecDNA presence.")
# Determine, per sample, tissue and whether any classification == 'ecDNA'
# Some samples may have multiple rows (multiple amplicons); we treat a sample as ecDNA-positive if any row has classification == 'ecDNA'
def is_ecdna(series):
    # handle possible nulls
    return (series == 'ecDNA').any()

per_sample = df.groupby('sample_name').agg(
    tissue_of_origin = ('tissue_of_origin', 'first'),
    any_ecDNA = ('classification', lambda x: (x == 'ecDNA').any())
).reset_index()

print("Per-sample aggregation: number of unique samples =", per_sample.shape[0])
print("Example per-sample rows:")
print(per_sample.head(8))

print("\nComputing per-tissue totals and ecDNA-positive counts/fractions.")
per_tissue = per_sample.groupby('tissue_of_origin').agg(
    n_samples = ('sample_name', 'nunique'),
    n_ecDNA = ('any_ecDNA', 'sum')
).reset_index()
per_tissue['fraction_ecDNA'] = per_tissue['n_ecDNA'] / per_tissue['n_samples']
per_tissue = per_tissue.sort_values('fraction_ecDNA', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)

# Format fraction as percent for display
per_tissue['percent_ecDNA'] = per_tissue['fraction_ecDNA'] * 100

print("\nPer-tissue summary (sorted by fraction of samples with any ecDNA):")
print(per_tissue.to_string(index=False))

# Save result variable as required
result = per_tissue.copy()
print("\nSaved result variable 'result' (first 10 rows):")
print(result.head(10))

# Print overall totals
total_samples = per_sample.shape[0]
total_ecdna_samples = per_sample['any_ecDNA'].sum()
print(f"\nOverall: {int(total_ecdna_samples)} / {int(total_samples)} samples have at least one ecDNA ({100.0 * total_ecdna_samples/total_samples:.2f}%).")

# Also output the number of tissues
print("\nNumber of tissues represented:", per_tissue.shape[0])

# For convenience, also print top 10 tissues by fraction with n samples
print("\nTop 10 tissues by fraction ecDNA (tissue, n_samples, n_ecDNA, percent):")
print(per_tissue[['tissue_of_origin','n_samples','n_ecDNA','percent_ecDNA']].head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Ensure the result is accessible
result = per_tissue

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

print("Research log: Re-querying using the file's actual column names.")

# Query using actual column names observed in the file
res = query_amplicons(select=['Sample name', 'Tissue of origin', 'Classification'], csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Top-level keys returned:", list(res.keys()))
print("Rows returned:", res.get('row_count_returned'))

rows = res.get('rows', [])
print("Number of rows retrieved:", len(rows))

# Build DataFrame
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("Columns in returned rows (first 30):", list(df.columns)[:30])

# Determine which column names to use (prefer normalized if present)
if 'sample_name' in df.columns and 'tissue_of_origin' in df.columns and 'classification' in df.columns:
    s_col, t_col, c_col = 'sample_name', 'tissue_of_origin', 'classification'
else:
    # Fall back to original column names
    s_col, t_col, c_col = 'Sample name', 'Tissue of origin', 'Classification'

print(f"Using columns: sample={s_col}, tissue={t_col}, classification={c_col}")

# Keep only relevant columns
df = df[[s_col, t_col, c_col]].copy()
df = df.rename(columns={s_col: 'sample_name', t_col: 'tissue_of_origin', c_col: 'classification'})
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Example rows:")
print(df.head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Normalize classification entries (some may be NaN)
df['classification'] = df['classification'].fillna('').astype(str)

# Aggregate per sample: a sample is ecDNA-positive if any of its rows has classification == 'ecDNA'
per_sample = df.groupby('sample_name').agg(
    tissue_of_origin = ('tissue_of_origin', lambda x: x.dropna().unique()[0] if len(x.dropna().unique())>0 else ''),
    any_ecDNA = ('classification', lambda x: (x == 'ecDNA').any())
).reset_index()

print("\nPer-sample aggregation: total unique samples =", per_sample.shape[0])
print("Example per-sample rows:")
print(per_sample.head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Compute per-tissue statistics
per_tissue = per_sample.groupby('tissue_of_origin').agg(
    n_samples = ('sample_name', 'nunique'),
    n_ecDNA = ('any_ecDNA', 'sum')
).reset_index()

per_tissue['fraction_ecDNA'] = per_tissue['n_ecDNA'] / per_tissue['n_samples']
per_tissue['percent_ecDNA'] = per_tissue['fraction_ecDNA'] * 100
per_tissue = per_tissue.sort_values('fraction_ecDNA', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)

print("\nPer-tissue summary (sorted by fraction with any ecDNA):")
print(per_tissue.to_string(index=False))

# Overall stats
total_samples = per_sample.shape[0]
total_ecdna_samples = int(per_sample['any_ecDNA'].sum())
overall_pct = 100.0 * total_ecdna_samples / total_samples if total_samples>0 else 0.0
print(f"\nOverall: {total_ecdna_samples} / {total_samples} samples have at least one ecDNA ({overall_pct:.2f}%).")

# Save result variable
result = per_tissue

print("\nTop 10 tissues by fraction ecDNA:")
print(per_tissue[['tissue_of_origin','n_samples','n_ecDNA','percent_ecDNA']].head(10).to_string(index=False))

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

# Query the CCLE dataset for classification and complexity_score
res = query_amplicons(select=['Classification','complexity_score'],
                      csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                      add_normalized_columns=True)

print("Raw query result type:", type(res))

# Try to coerce result into a pandas DataFrame if it isn't already
if isinstance(res, dict):
    print("Result is a dict; keys:", list(res.keys()))
    # attempt to locate tabular data within common keys
    for k in ['rows','data','results','records','rows_ret']:
        if k in res:
            df = pd.DataFrame(res[k])
            break
    else:
        # fallback: try to build DataFrame directly from dict
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(res)
        except Exception as e:
            print("Could not convert dict to DataFrame:", e)
            df = None

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
import itertools
import math

print("Reusing previously obtained 'res' from query_amplicons; if not present, re-querying...")

# Attempt to reuse res from the environment; if not present, re-run query
try:
    res
    print("Using existing 'res' variable.")
except NameError:
    print("'res' not found; querying now.")
    res = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Step 2: Convert rows to DataFrame
print("\nStep 2: Converting res['rows'] to DataFrame...")
rows = res.get('rows', None)
if rows is None:
    raise RuntimeError("No 'rows' key in query result.")
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("Initial DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns present (sample):", df.columns.tolist()[:20])
print("First 5 rows:")
print(df.head(5).to_string(index=False))

# Normalize columns
if 'classification' not in df.columns:
    if 'Classification' in df.columns:
        df['classification'] = df['Classification']
    else:
        raise RuntimeError("No classification column found.")
if 'complexity_score' not in df.columns:
    if 'Complexity score' in df.columns:
        # Coerce complexity score to numeric; handle various NA representations
        df['complexity_score'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Complexity score'], errors='coerce')
    else:
        raise RuntimeError("No Complexity score column found.")

print("\nAfter normalization, columns include 'classification' and 'complexity_score'.")
print("Number of total rows:", df.shape[0])
print("Non-null complexity_score count:", df['complexity_score'].notnull().sum())

# Step 3: Inspect counts per classification and missingness
print("\nStep 3: Group counts by classification (including NaNs):")
counts_all = df['classification'].value_counts(dropna=False)
print(counts_all.to_string())

# Filter to rows with non-null classification and complexity_score
df_filtered = df[df['classification'].notnull() & df['complexity_score'].notnull()].copy()
print("\nAfter filtering to non-null classification and complexity_score:")
print("Rows remaining:", df_filtered.shape[0])
print("Counts by classification (filtered):")
group_counts = df_filtered['classification'].value_counts()
print(group_counts.to_string())

# List unique classifications
groups = sorted(df_filtered['classification'].unique())
print("\nUnique classifications present:", groups)

# Step 4: Descriptive statistics by classification
print("\nStep 4: Descriptive statistics by classification:")
desc_stats = []
for g in groups:
    vals = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==g, 'complexity_score'].astype(float)
    n = vals.size
    mean = vals.mean()
    median = vals.median()
    std = vals.std(ddof=1)
    q1 = vals.quantile(0.25)
    q3 = vals.quantile(0.75)
    iqr = q3 - q1
    desc_stats.append({'classification': g, 'N': n, 'mean': mean, 'median': median, 'std': std, 'Q1': q1, 'Q3': q3, 'IQR': iqr})
desc_df = pd.DataFrame(desc_stats).sort_values('classification').reset_index(drop=True)
print(desc_df.to_string(index=False))

# Step 5: Kruskal-Wallis test across groups
print("\nStep 5: Kruskal-Wallis test (nonparametric) across groups:")
group_values = [df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==g, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values for g in groups]
# Ensure at least two groups with >0 samples
if sum([len(v)>0 for v in group_values]) < 2:
    print("Not enough groups with data to run Kruskal-Wallis.")
    kruskal_res = None
else:
    kruskal_stat, kruskal_p = stats.kruskal(*group_values)
    kruskal_res = {'H': kruskal_stat, 'p': kruskal_p}
    print("Kruskal-Wallis H = {:.4f}, p = {:.4e}".format(kruskal_stat, kruskal_p))

# Step 6: Pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction
print("\nStep 6: Pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests (two-sided) with Bonferroni correction:")
pairs = list(itertools.combinations(groups, 2))
pair_results = []
for a,b in pairs:
    va = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==a, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values
    vb = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==b, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values
    # require at least one sample in each
    if len(va) < 1 or len(vb) < 1:
        print(f"Skipping pair {a} vs {b} due to insufficient samples.")
        continue
    # Use Mann-Whitney U, two-sided
    try:
        ustat, pval = stats.mannwhitneyu(va, vb, alternative='two-sided')
    except Exception as e:
        # fallback: use rank-sum (equivalent)
        print("mannwhitneyu failed:", e)
        ustat, pval = (np.nan, np.nan)
    pair_results.append({'group1': a, 'group2': b, 'U': ustat, 'p': pval, 'n1': len(va), 'n2': len(vb)})
# Apply Bonferroni correction
m = len(pair_results)
for pr in pair_results:
    rawp = pr['p']
    padj = min(rawp * m, 1.0) if not math.isnan(rawp) else np.nan
    pr['p_adj_bonf'] = padj

pair_df = pd.DataFrame(pair_results)
if pair_df.empty:
    print("No pairwise tests performed.")
else:
    print("\nPairwise test results (raw p and Bonferroni-adjusted p):")
    print(pair_df[['group1','group2','n1','n2','U','p','p_adj_bonf']].to_string(index=False))

# Save outputs for review
desc_df.to_csv("complexity_by_classification_stats.csv", index=False)
pair_df.to_csv("complexity_pairwise_tests.csv", index=False)
print("\nSaved descriptive stats to complexity_by_classification_stats.csv and pairwise tests to complexity_pairwise_tests.csv")

# Print summary conclusion placeholder
print("\nSummary placeholder: Inspect results above. If Kruskal-Wallis p < 0.05, there is evidence of overall differences. Check pairwise adjusted p-values for which groups differ significantly.")

# Print variable names for saved results
print("\nVariables available in environment: df (full), df_filtered (filtered), desc_df, pair_df, kruskal_res")

# Print a short sample of df_filtered
print("\nSample filtered rows (first 6):")
print(df_filtered[['classification','complexity_score']].head(6).to_string(index=False))
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
import itertools
import math

# Re-query or reuse res
try:
    res
except NameError:
    res = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

rows = res.get('rows', [])
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)

# Normalize columns
if 'classification' not in df.columns and 'Classification' in df.columns:
    df['classification'] = df['Classification']
if 'complexity_score' not in df.columns and 'Complexity score' in df.columns:
    df['complexity_score'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Complexity score'], errors='coerce')

# Filter to non-null classification and complexity_score
df_filtered = df[df['classification'].notnull() & df['complexity_score'].notnull()].copy()

# Groups of interest (present in data)
groups = sorted(df_filtered['classification'].unique())

# Descriptive stats
desc = []
for g in groups:
    vals = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==g, 'complexity_score'].astype(float)
    desc.append({
        'classification': g,
        'N': int(vals.size),
        'mean': float(vals.mean()),
        'median': float(vals.median()),
        'std': float(vals.std(ddof=1)),
        'Q1': float(vals.quantile(0.25)),
        'Q3': float(vals.quantile(0.75)),
        'IQR': float(vals.quantile(0.75) - vals.quantile(0.25))
    })
desc_df = pd.DataFrame(desc).sort_values('classification').reset_index(drop=True)

# Kruskal-Wallis
group_values = [df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==g, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values for g in groups]
kruskal_stat, kruskal_p = stats.kruskal(*group_values)

# Pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction
pairs = list(itertools.combinations(groups, 2))
pair_results = []
for a,b in pairs:
    va = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==a, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values
    vb = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']==b, 'complexity_score'].astype(float).values
    # require at least one sample in each
    if len(va) < 1 or len(vb) < 1:
        continue
    ustat, pval = stats.mannwhitneyu(va, vb, alternative='two-sided')
    pair_results.append({'group1': a, 'group2': b, 'n1': len(va), 'n2': len(vb), 'U': float(ustat), 'p_raw': float(pval)})

m = len(pair_results)
for pr in pair_results:
    padj = min(pr['p_raw'] * m, 1.0)
    pr['p_adj_bonf'] = padj
pair_df = pd.DataFrame(pair_results)

# Determine significant pairwise comparisons after Bonferroni correction
sig_pairs = pair_df[pair_df['p_adj_bonf'] < 0.05].copy()
# Add direction based on medians
def median_diff(row):
    m1 = desc_df.loc[desc_df['classification']==row['group1'],'median'].values[0]
    m2 = desc_df.loc[desc_df['classification']==row['group2'],'median'].values[0]
    if m1 > m2:
        return f"{row['group1']} > {row['group2']}"
    elif m1 < m2:
        return f"{row['group1']} < {row['group2']}"
    else:
        return "equal medians"
if not sig_pairs.empty:
    sig_pairs['direction'] = sig_pairs.apply(median_diff, axis=1)

# Print concise outputs
print("Counts by classification (filtered to non-null complexity_score):")
print(df_filtered['classification'].value_counts().to_string())
print("\nDescriptive statistics (per classification):")
print(desc_df.to_string(index=False))

print("\nKruskal-Wallis test (H, p):")
print(f"H = {kruskal_stat:.4f}, p = {kruskal_p:.4e}")

print("\nPairwise Mann-Whitney U tests (raw p, Bonferroni-adjusted p):")
if pair_df.empty:
    print("No pairwise comparisons performed.")
else:
    display_df = pair_df[['group1','group2','n1','n2','U','p_raw','p_adj_bonf']].copy()
    # round numeric columns for concise display
    for col in ['U','p_raw','p_adj_bonf']:
        display_df[col] = display_df[col].map(lambda x: round(x,6))
    print(display_df.to_string(index=False))

if not sig_pairs.empty:
    print("\nSignificant pairwise differences after Bonferroni correction (p_adj_bonf < 0.05):")
    print(sig_pairs[['group1','group2','n1','n2','p_raw','p_adj_bonf','direction']].to_string(index=False))
else:
    print("\nNo pairwise comparisons were significant after Bonferroni correction (alpha=0.05).")

# Save results to CSVs (optional)
desc_df.to_csv("complexity_by_classification_stats.csv", index=False)
pair_df.to_csv("complexity_pairwise_tests.csv", index=False)

# Expose key results for potential further inspection
kruskal_res = {'H': float(kruskal_stat), 'p': float(kruskal_p)}
print("\nSaved results to CSV. Variables: desc_df, pair_df, kruskal_res, df_filtered")
</execute>

- Source: CCLE amplicon records from the provided dataset.
- Included records: Only amplicons with non-missing Classification and Complexity score.
- Group sample sizes:

  - Complex-non-cyclic: N = 196
  - BFB: N = 114

- Linear: N=550, mean=0.581, median=0.636, IQR≈0.434
- ecDNA: N=297, mean=0.841, median=0.687, IQR≈0.411
- Complex-non-cyclic: N=196, mean=1.262, median=1.189, IQR≈0.613
- BFB: N=114, mean=1.064, median=1.021, IQR≈0.331

Statistical tests:
- Kruskal-Wallis (overall, nonparametric): H = 367.9451, p ≈ 1.94e-79 — strong evidence of overall differences in complexity score across classifications.
- Pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests (two-sided), Bonferroni-adjusted for multiple comparisons (6 pairwise tests):
  - All six pairwise comparisons were significant after Bonferroni correction (adjusted p < 0.001 in all cases).
  - Direction by median (rank ordering of medians): Complex-non-cyclic (highest median) > BFB > ecDNA > Linear (lowest median).
    - Example significant comparisons (adjusted p-values):
      - Complex-non-cyclic vs Linear: p_adj ≈ 4.84e-58
      - Complex-non-cyclic vs ecDNA: p_adj ≈ 1.47e-26

      - ecDNA vs Linear: p_adj ≈ 1.45e-11
      - BFB vs ecDNA: p_adj ≈ 4.92e-15
      - BFB vs Complex-non-cyclic: p_adj ≈ 7.57e-04

Interpretation:
- Complexity scores differ significantly among amplicon classifications. Complex-non-cyclic amplicons have the highest complexity scores on average/median, followed by BFB, then ecDNA, with Linear amplicons showing the lowest complexity scores.

<execute>
# Step 3 & 4: Compute correlations and produce scatter plot
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy import stats
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
import os

# Use df_clean from previous step
df = df_clean.copy()
print("Using cleaned dataframe with {} rows".format(len(df)))
print("Counts by classification:\n", df['classification'].value_counts().to_string())

# Prepare log-transformed columns
df['log10_size'] = np.log10(df['size_bp'])
df['log10_maxcopy'] = np.log10(df['max_copy_number'])

# Function to compute correlations and return a dict
def compute_corrs(subdf):
    res = {}
    # Raw Pearson
    r,p = stats.pearsonr(subdf['size_bp'], subdf['max_copy_number'])
    res['pearson_r_raw'] = r
    res['pearson_p_raw'] = p
    # Raw Spearman
    rho, p_s = stats.spearmanr(subdf['size_bp'], subdf['max_copy_number'])
    res['spearman_rho_raw'] = rho
    res['spearman_p_raw'] = p_s
    # Log Pearson
    r_log, p_log = stats.pearsonr(subdf['log10_size'], subdf['log10_maxcopy'])
    res['pearson_r_log'] = r_log
    res['pearson_p_log'] = p_log
    # Log Spearman
    rho_log, p_s_log = stats.spearmanr(subdf['log10_size'], subdf['log10_maxcopy'])
    res['spearman_rho_log'] = rho_log
    res['spearman_p_log'] = p_s_log
    return res

# Compute for overall, ecDNA, BFB
results = {}
results['overall'] = compute_corrs(df)
for cls in df['classification'].unique():
    sub = df[df['classification']==cls]
    results[cls] = compute_corrs(sub)

# Print results clearly
print("\nCorrelation results (pearson r, p ; spearman rho, p) RAW and LOG10-transformed:")
for key, vals in results.items():
    print("\n== {} (n={}) ==".format(key, len(df) if key=='overall' else len(df[df['classification']==key])))
    print("Pearson (raw): r = {:.4f}, p = {:.2e}".format(vals['pearson_r_raw'], vals['pearson_p_raw']))
    print("Spearman (raw): rho = {:.4f}, p = {:.2e}".format(vals['spearman_rho_raw'], vals['spearman_p_raw']))
    print("Pearson (log10): r = {:.4f}, p = {:.2e}".format(vals['pearson_r_log'], vals['pearson_p_log']))
    print("Spearman (log10): rho = {:.4f}, p = {:.2e}".format(vals['spearman_rho_log'], vals['spearman_p_log']))

# Save results for later inspection
corr_results = results
print("\nSaved correlation results to variable 'corr_results'")

# Create scatter plot on log10-log10 scale with regression lines per class
sns.set(style="whitegrid", context='talk', palette='dark')
plt.figure(figsize=(8,6))
ax = plt.gca()

# Plot points, jitter small for visualization if needed
sns.scatterplot(data=df, x='log10_size', y='log10_maxcopy', hue='classification', alpha=0.7, s=60, edgecolor='k')

# Fit and plot regression lines on log-log for each class
classes = df['classification'].unique()
colors = sns.color_palette(n_colors=len(classes))
for i,cls in enumerate(classes):
    sub = df[df['classification']==cls]
    # Fit linear regression on log-log
    slope, intercept, r_value, p_value, std_err = stats.linregress(sub['log10_size'], sub['log10_maxcopy'])
    xs = np.linspace(df['log10_size'].min(), df['log10_size'].max(), 100)
    ys = intercept + slope*xs
    plt.plot(xs, ys, color=colors[i], linestyle='--', label=f"{cls} fit (slope={slope:.2f})")

# Labels and legend
plt.xlabel('log10(Captured interval length, bp)')
plt.ylabel('log10(Feature maximum copy number)')
plt.title('ecDNA vs BFB: log10(size) vs log10(max copy number)')
plt.legend(loc='best', fontsize='medium')
plt.tight_layout()

# Save plot
out_dir = 'results_plots'
os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
plot_path = os.path.join(out_dir, 'ecDNA_BFB_size_vs_maxcopy_loglog.png')
plt.savefig(plot_path, dpi=300)
plt.close()
print(f"\nSaved scatter plot with regression lines to: {plot_path}")

# Also save a CSV summary of the cleaned data and correlations
csv_path = os.path.join(out_dir, 'ecDNA_BFB_cleaned_data.csv')
df.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)
print(f"Saved cleaned data to: {csv_path}")

print("\nStep 3 & 4 completed: correlations computed and plot saved.")

# Helper to extract oncogene strings robustly from the returned structure
def extract_oncogene_strings(result):
    """
    Accepts the raw result from query_amplicons and returns a list of oncogene-string values (as they appear).
    Handles dict with 'rows' or list/dict or pandas DataFrame.
    """
    values = []
    # If dict with rows
    if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
        for r in result['rows']:
            # Try common keys
            for key in ['Oncogenes', 'oncogenes', 'Oncogene', 'ONCOGENES']:
                if key in r:
                    values.append(r[key])
                    break
            else:
                # If none of the keys found, append None placeholder
                values.append(None)
    else:
        # Try to convert to DataFrame
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(result)
            # try to find a column with oncogene-like name
            col = None
            for candidate in ['oncogenes', 'Oncogenes', 'ONCOGENES', 'Oncogene']:
                if candidate in df.columns:
                    col = candidate
                    break
            if col is None:
                # fallback: use any column that looks like gene list (string dtype)
                for c in df.columns:
                    if df[c].dtype == object:
                        col = c
                        break
            if col is not None:
                values = df[col].tolist()
            else:
                values = [None] * len(df)
        except Exception as e:
            # final fallback
            values = []
    return values

# Parse gene-list strings into normalized gene symbols
def parse_oncogene_field_list(field_values):
    """
    field_values: list of strings (or Nones) containing gene lists; can be separated by ',', ';', '|' or whitespace.
    Returns set of normalized gene symbols (upper-case, trimmed).
    """
    genes = set()
    for v in field_values:
        if v is None:
            continue
        # skip NaN
        try:
            if isinstance(v, float) and math.isnan(v):
                continue
        except:
            pass
        s = str(v)
        if s.strip() == '':
            continue
        # Common separators
        parts = []
        if ';' in s:
            parts = s.split(';')
        elif '|' in s:
            parts = s.split('|')
        elif ',' in s:
            parts = s.split(',')
        else:
            # split on whitespace if looks like space-separated
            parts = s.split()
        for p in parts:
            p2 = p.strip()
            if p2 == '' or p2.upper() == 'NAN':
                continue
            # remove trailing/leading punctuation
            p2 = p2.strip(" [](){}'")
            genes.add(p2.upper())
    return genes

# Extract and parse for ecDNA
fields_ec = extract_oncogene_strings(result_ec)
print("\nExtracted oncogene field values for ecDNA: sample 10 entries:")
for i,v in enumerate(fields_ec[:10]):
    print(i+1, ":", v)
oncogenes_ec = parse_oncogene_field_list(fields_ec)
print("Number of unique oncogenes found in ecDNA results:", len(oncogenes_ec))

# Extract and parse for BFB
fields_bfb = extract_oncogene_strings(result_bfb)
print("\nExtracted oncogene field values for BFB: sample 10 entries:")
for i,v in enumerate(fields_bfb[:10]):
    print(i+1, ":", v)
oncogenes_bfb = parse_oncogene_field_list(fields_bfb)
print("Number of unique oncogenes found in BFB results:", len(oncogenes_bfb))

# Step 3: compute intersection
intersection = sorted(list(oncogenes_ec.intersection(oncogenes_bfb)))
print("\n--- Step 3: Intersection computed ---")
print("Number of shared oncogenes between ecDNA and BFB:", len(intersection))
print("Shared oncogenes (sorted):")
print(intersection)

# Save outputs into variables so they are available in the environment
result_summary = {
    'oncogenes_ec_count': len(oncogenes_ec),
    'oncogenes_bfb_count': len(oncogenes_bfb),
    'intersection_count': len(intersection),
    'intersection_list': intersection
}

print("\nResult summary saved to result_summary. Full summary:")
print(result_summary)

# Step 3-5: Inspect rows and compute samples containing both ecDNA and BFB
# Using 'result' from the earlier query (should be present in the environment)

print("Step 3: Inspecting available fields in a sample row to confirm field names...")
rows = result.get('rows', [])
if not rows:
    print("No rows found in result. Exiting.")
else:
    print("Example row keys:", list(rows[0].keys())[:30])

# Determine normalized field names (prefer 'sample_name' and 'classification' if present)
sample_field = 'sample_name' if 'sample_name' in rows[0] else 'Sample name'
class_field = 'classification' if 'classification' in rows[0] else 'Classification'
print(f"Using sample identifier field: {sample_field}")
print(f"Using classification field: {class_field}")

# Build sets of samples for each classification
ecdna_samples = set()
bfb_samples = set()

for r in rows:
    samp = r.get(sample_field) or r.get('Sample name')
    cl = r.get(class_field) or r.get('Classification')
    if samp is None or cl is None:
        continue
    # Normalize values
    samp_norm = str(samp).strip()
    cl_norm = str(cl).strip().lower()
    if cl_norm == 'ecdna':
        ecdna_samples.add(samp_norm)
    elif cl_norm == 'bfb':
        bfb_samples.add(samp_norm)

# Compute intersection
both = sorted(list(ecdna_samples.intersection(bfb_samples)))

# Print summary and lists
print("\nSummary:")
print(f"Unique samples with ecDNA: {len(ecdna_samples)}")
print(f"Unique samples with BFB:   {len(bfb_samples)}")
print(f"Samples with both ecDNA and BFB: {len(both)}\n")

# Print the list of samples with both (or a message if none)
if both:
    print("Samples containing both ecDNA and BFB amplifications (sorted):")
    for s in both:
        print(s)
else:
    print("No samples contain both ecDNA and BFB amplifications.")

# Save outputs for downstream use / verification
ecdna_list = sorted(ecdna_samples)
bfb_list = sorted(bfb_samples)
both_list = both

print("\nSaved lists to variables: ecdna_list, bfb_list, both_list")

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats

print("Step 3: Computing summary statistics and running Mann-Whitney U test...")

# Re-query to ensure data present
result = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'],
                         select=['Classification','Captured interval length'],
                         csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv")
rows = result.get('rows', [])
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)

# Use normalized column names
length_col = 'captured_interval_length' if 'captured_interval_length' in df.columns else 'Captured interval length'
class_col = 'classification' if 'classification' in df.columns else 'Classification'

ecdna = df.loc[df[class_col] == 'ecDNA', length_col].dropna().astype(float).values
bfb = df.loc[df[class_col] == 'BFB', length_col].dropna().astype(float).values

# Compute summary stats
def summarize(arr):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=float)
    n = arr.size
    mean = arr.mean() if n>0 else np.nan
    median = np.median(arr) if n>0 else np.nan
    std = arr.std(ddof=1) if n>1 else np.nan
    q1 = np.percentile(arr,25) if n>0 else np.nan
    q3 = np.percentile(arr,75) if n>0 else np.nan
    iqr = q3 - q1 if n>0 else np.nan
    return {'n': n, 'mean': mean, 'median': median, 'std': std, 'q1': q1, 'q3': q3, 'IQR': iqr}

ecdna_summary = summarize(ecdna)
bfb_summary = summarize(bfb)

print("\necDNA summary (captured interval length, bp):")
print(ecdna_summary)
print("\nBFB summary (captured interval length, bp):")
print(bfb_summary)

# Mann-Whitney U test (one-sided: ecDNA > BFB)
# Use alternative='greater' to test whether values in ecDNA tend to be larger than BFB
mw_stat, mw_p = stats.mannwhitneyu(ecdna, bfb, alternative='greater')
print(f"\nMann-Whitney U test (alternative ecDNA > BFB): U = {mw_stat:.3f}, p-value = {mw_p:.4g}")

# Additionally compute two-sided p for context
mw_stat2, mw_p2 = stats.mannwhitneyu(ecdna, bfb, alternative='two-sided')
print(f"Mann-Whitney U test (two-sided): U = {mw_stat2:.3f}, p-value = {mw_p2:.4g}")

# Compute Cliff's delta as nonparametric effect size
def cliffs_delta(x, y):
    # compute pairwise comparisons
    n_x = len(x)
    n_y = len(y)
    # efficient vectorized calculation
    diffs = np.subtract.outer(x, y)
    greater = np.sum(diffs > 0)
    lesser = np.sum(diffs < 0)
    delta = (greater - lesser) / (n_x * n_y)
    return delta

delta = cliffs_delta(ecdna, bfb)
print(f"\nCliff's delta (ecDNA vs BFB): {delta:.4f} (range -1 to 1; positive means ecDNA > BFB)")

# Save outputs (required) and print brief summary
result_summary = {
    'ecdna_summary': ecdna_summary,
    'bfb_summary': bfb_summary,
    'mw_U_greater': mw_stat,
    'mw_p_greater': mw_p,
    'mw_U_two_sided': mw_stat2,
    'mw_p_two_sided': mw_p2,
    'cliffs_delta': delta
}

print("\nSaved result_summary variable with computed statistics.")

# Print sample sizes explicitly
print(f"\nSample sizes: ecDNA n = {ecdna_summary['n']}, BFB n = {bfb_summary['n']}")

# For transparency, print medians in kb (kilobases) as well
print(f"\nMedians: ecDNA = {ecdna_summary['median']:.0f} bp ({ecdna_summary['median']/1000:.1f} kb), "
      f"BFB = {bfb_summary['median']:.0f} bp ({bfb_summary['median']/1000:.1f} kb)")

# End of step 3
print("\nStep 3 completed: summary statistics and statistical test computed.")
</execute>

<solution>
No — the data do not support that ecDNA amplifications are larger than BFB on average. If anything, BFB amplicons are slightly larger.

Key results (captured interval length in base pairs):
- Sample sizes: ecDNA n = 297, BFB n = 114
- Means: ecDNA mean ≈ 2,096,639 bp; BFB mean ≈ 2,271,737 bp
- Medians: ecDNA median = 985,738 bp (≈ 986 kb); BFB median = 1,276,367 bp (≈ 1,276 kb)
- IQR: ecDNA IQR ≈ 1,972,563 bp; BFB IQR ≈ 2,425,372 bp
- Mann–Whitney U test:
  - One-sided (alternative ecDNA > BFB): U = 14775.0, p = 0.9772 — no evidence that ecDNA > BFB.
  - Two-sided: U = 14775.0, p ≈ 0.0458 — overall distributions differ at about the 0.05 level (direction indicates ecDNA < BFB).
- Cliff’s delta (effect size, ecDNA vs BFB): -0.1272 (small negative effect; negative means ecDNA values tend to be smaller).

Interpretation and caveats:
- Medians (and the negative Cliff’s delta) show ecDNA tends to be smaller than BFB in this dataset; means are similar but both classes have large variance and some very large outliers.
- The one-sided test (ecDNA > BFB) strongly fails; the two-sided test is marginal (p≈0.046) and indicates a small difference overall, but effect size is small (Cliff’s delta ≈ −0.13).
- Because distributions are skewed with high variance and outliers, the median and nonparametric tests are more informative than the mean.
- If you’d like, I can (a) plot the distributions (boxplots / density), (b) repeat the comparison stratified by tissue of origin or by removing extreme outliers, or (c compute bootstrap confidence intervals for medians — tell me which you prefer.
</solution>

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
import json

# Re-run the query to ensure a fresh variable (should be same results)
query_result = query_amplicons(classification='ecDNA',
                              feature_max_copy_number_min=21,
                              csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                              add_normalized_columns=True)

rows = query_result.get('rows', [])

def clean_oncogenes_field(val):
    """
    Try to parse the oncogenes field which is a string representation of a list
    containing items like \"'GENE'\". Return a cleaned Python list of gene symbols.
    """
    if val is None:
        return []
    # If already a list, process entries
    if isinstance(val, list):
        items = val
    else:
        # Try to literal_eval; fallback to simple cleaning
        try:
            items = ast.literal_eval(val)
        except Exception:
            # Try to strip leading/trailing brackets and split by comma
            s = val.strip()
            if s.startswith('[') and s.endswith(']'):
                s = s[1:-1]
            parts = [p.strip() for p in s.split(',') if p.strip()]
            items = parts
    # Now strip extra quotes around each item
    cleaned = []
    for it in items:
        if it is None:
            continue
        s = str(it).strip()
        # remove surrounding single or double quotes
        if (s.startswith('"') and s.endswith('"')) or (s.startswith("'") and s.endswith("'")):
            s = s[1:-1]
        # remove any remaining surrounding quotes
        if (s.startswith('"') and s.endswith('"')) or (s.startswith("'") and s.endswith("'")):
            s = s[1:-1]
        cleaned.append(s)
    return cleaned

# Extract relevant fields into structured records
high_copy_ecdna_records = []
for r in rows:
    # Use normalized keys present in each row (lowercase underscores)
    sample = r.get('sample_name') or r.get('Sample name') or r.get('Sample') or r.get('sample')
    aa_num = r.get('aa_amplicon_number') or r.get('AA amplicon number')
    tissue = r.get('tissue_of_origin') or r.get('Tissue of origin') or r.get('tissue')
    onc_field = r.get('oncogenes') or r.get('Oncogenes') or r.get('Oncogene')
    max_cn = r.get('feature_maximum_copy_number') or r.get('Feature maximum copy number') or r.get('feature_max_copy_number')
    # Clean oncogenes
    oncogenes_clean = clean_oncogenes_field(onc_field)
    try:
        max_cn_val = float(max_cn) if max_cn is not None else None
    except Exception:
        max_cn_val = None
    rec = {
        'sample_name': sample,
        'aa_amplicon_number': aa_num,
        'tissue_of_origin': tissue,
        'oncogenes': oncogenes_clean,
        'feature_maximum_copy_number': max_cn_val
    }
    high_copy_ecdna_records.append(rec)

# Save and print result
print("Saved extracted records to variable 'high_copy_ecdna_records'.")
print("Total records extracted:", len(high_copy_ecdna_records))

# Sort by copy number descending (None values last)
high_copy_ecdna_records_sorted = sorted(high_copy_ecdna_records, key=lambda x: (-(x['feature_maximum_copy_number'] or -1)))
print("Saved sorted list to 'high_copy_ecdna_records_sorted'.")

# Print the top 50 records (or all if fewer)
max_display = 200
print("\nTop records (sample_name | aa_amplicon_number | tissue | max_CN | oncogenes):")
for i, rec in enumerate(high_copy_ecdna_records_sorted[:max_display]):
    print(f"{i+1:3d}. {rec['sample_name']} | amp#{rec['aa_amplicon_number']} | {rec['tissue_of_origin']} | {rec['feature_maximum_copy_number']:.3f} | {rec['oncogenes']}")

# Also print number of unique samples represented
unique_samples = sorted(set(r['sample_name'] for r in high_copy_ecdna_records_sorted if r['sample_name']))
print(f"\nNumber of unique samples with ecDNA feature_maximum_copy_number > 20: {len(unique_samples)}")
print("Sample list (unique):")
for s in unique_samples:
    print(" -", s)

# Print done
print("\nStep 3 completed: records extracted, cleaned, and sorted.")
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import json

print("Step: Querying amplicon table (CCLE.csv) for relevant columns...")

# Save and print the query output
result = query_amplicons(
    select=['classification','captured_interval_length','genomic_location','oncogenes','all_genes','sample_id','key','feature_max_copy_number'],
    csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv'
)
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'. Now printing a brief summary of the returned object:")

# Print the raw result (truncated if large)
try:
    # If result is a dict-like container with rows
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        print("Result is dict with keys:", list(result.keys()))
        if 'rows' in result:
            rows = result['rows']
            print(f"Number of rows returned: {len(rows)}")
            # show first 3 rows
            print("First 3 rows (truncated):")
            print(json.dumps(rows[:3], indent=2) if len(rows)>0 else "No rows")
            df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
        else:
            # print top-level content
            print(json.dumps(result, indent=2)[:1000])
            # try to convert to DataFrame if possible
            try:
                df = pd.DataFrame(result)
            except Exception as e:
                raise
    elif isinstance(result, list):
        print(f"Result is a list with {len(result)} items. Showing first 3 items:")
        print(json.dumps(result[:3], indent=2))
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
    elif isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
        print("Result is a pandas DataFrame with shape", result.shape)
        df = result
    else:
        print("Result type:", type(result))
        # attempt to convert to DataFrame
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error while interpreting query result:", str(e))
    raise

print("\nDataFrame columns:", list(df.columns))

# Normalize expected column names to consistent lower-case snake_case if needed
# The query tool should have provided 'classification' and 'captured_interval_length' columns (normalized).
# Try common variants if not present.
possible_size_cols = ['captured_interval_length', 'Captured_interval_length', 'captured_interval_length_bp', 'captured_interval_length_bp']
size_col = None
for c in possible_size_cols:
    if c in df.columns:
        size_col = c
        break
# try normalized alias
if size_col is None:
    # try to find any column containing 'captur' and 'length' keywords
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'captur' in c.lower() and 'length' in c.lower():
            size_col = c
            break

if size_col is None:
    raise ValueError("Could not find a column representing captured interval length in query result. Available columns: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

print("Using size column:", size_col)

# Ensure numeric
df[size_col] = pd.to_numeric(df[size_col], errors='coerce')

# Check classification column name
class_col = None
for c in ['classification','Classification','classification_norm']:
    if c in df.columns:
        class_col = c
        break
if class_col is None:
    # attempt best guess: find a column with a small set of unique values like ecDNA,BFB,Linear,...
    for c in df.columns:
        uniq = df[c].dropna().unique()
        if len(uniq) > 0 and set([str(u).lower() for u in uniq]).intersection({'ecdna','ecdna','bfb','linear','complex-non-cyclic','complex_non-cyclic','complex-non_cyclic'}):
            class_col = c
            break

if class_col is None:
    raise ValueError("Could not find classification column. Available columns: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

print("Using classification column:", class_col)

# Now compute largest amplicon by size per classification
print("Computing largest amplicon (by captured interval length) per classification...")

# Drop rows without classification or size
df_subset = df[[class_col, size_col] + [c for c in ['key','sample_id','oncogenes','all_genes','genomic_location','feature_max_copy_number'] if c in df.columns]].copy()
df_subset = df_subset.dropna(subset=[class_col, size_col])

# Standardize classification strings
df_subset[class_col] = df_subset[class_col].astype(str)

# For safety, trim whitespace
df_subset[class_col] = df_subset[class_col].str.strip()

# Group and find idxmax
idx = df_subset.groupby(class_col)[size_col].idxmax()

largest_per_class = df_subset.loc[idx].reset_index(drop=True)

print("\nLargest amplicon per classification (computed):")
print(largest_per_class.to_dict(orient='records'))

# Save final dataframe to variable for inspection
largest_per_class_df = largest_per_class

# Print a concise human-readable table
print("\nConcise results:")
for _, row in largest_per_class_df.iterrows():
    print(f"- Classification: {row[class_col]}")
    print(f"  Key: {row.get('key','')}, Sample: {row.get('sample_id','')}")
    print(f"  Captured interval length: {row[size_col]}")
    if 'oncogenes' in row.index and pd.notna(row['oncogenes']):
        print(f"  Oncogenes: {row['oncogenes']}")
    if 'all_genes' in row.index and pd.notna(row['all_genes']):
        print(f"  All genes: {row['all_genes']}")
    if 'genomic_location' in row.index and pd.notna(row['genomic_location']):
        print(f"  Genomic location: {row['genomic_location']}")
    if 'feature_max_copy_number' in row.index and pd.notna(row['feature_max_copy_number']):
        print(f"  Feature max copy number: {row['feature_max_copy_number']}")
    print("")

# Print completion marker
print("Query and computation completed. Variables available: result, df, df_subset, largest_per_class_df")
