from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import json

print("Step: Querying full CCLE amplicon table (no select) ...")
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv')

print("Saved query result in variable 'result'. Inspecting type and keys...")

# Inspect result structure and convert to DataFrame
if isinstance(result, dict):
    if 'rows' in result:
        rows = result['rows']
        df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    else:
        # try to convert dict directly
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
elif isinstance(result, list):
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)
elif isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result
else:
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)

print("DataFrame loaded. Shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns available:", list(df.columns))

# Map column names as observed in the dataset
# Based on dataset observation, expected columns include these exact names:
col_class = None
col_size = None
candidates_class = ['Classification','classification']
candidates_size = ['Captured interval length','captured_interval_length','Captured interval length (bp)']

for c in candidates_class:
    if c in df.columns:
        col_class = c
        break
# fallback: find a column whose name contains 'classif'
if col_class is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'classif' in c.lower():
            col_class = c
            break

for c in candidates_size:
    if c in df.columns:
        col_size = c
        break
# fallback: find a column containing both 'captur' and 'length'
if col_size is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'captur' in c.lower() and 'length' in c.lower():
            col_size = c
            break

if col_class is None or col_size is None:
    raise ValueError(f"Required columns not found. Found class: {col_class}, size: {col_size}. Available columns: {list(df.columns)}")

print(f"Using classification column: '{col_class}' and size column: '{col_size}'")

# Select useful metadata columns if present
meta_cols = []
for name in ['Feature ID','AA amplicon number','Sample name','Location','Oncogenes','All genes','Feature maximum copy number','Feature median copy number']:
    if name in df.columns:
        meta_cols.append(name)

print("Metadata columns to include:", meta_cols)

# Prepare subset and clean
df_subset = df[[col_class, col_size] + meta_cols].copy()
# Convert size to numeric
df_subset[col_size] = pd.to_numeric(df_subset[col_size], errors='coerce')
# Drop rows missing classification or size
df_subset = df_subset.dropna(subset=[col_class, col_size])

# Strip whitespace from classification
df_subset[col_class] = df_subset[col_class].astype(str).str.strip()

print("Number of rows with valid class and size:", df_subset.shape[0])

print("\nComputing largest amplicon (by captured interval length) per classification...")
idx = df_subset.groupby(col_class)[col_size].idxmax()
largest_per_class_df = df_subset.loc[idx].reset_index(drop=True)

# Save result variable and print it
print("\nLargest amplicon per classification (raw dicts):")
largest_dicts = largest_per_class_df.to_dict(orient='records')
print(json.dumps(largest_dicts, indent=2))

# Also print a concise human-readable summary
print("\nConcise summary by classification:")
for i, row in largest_per_class_df.iterrows():
    cls = row[col_class]
    size = row[col_size]
    fid = row.get('Feature ID', '')
    aa_num = row.get('AA amplicon number','')
    sample = row.get('Sample name','')
    loc = row.get('Location','')
    onc = row.get('Oncogenes','')
    allg = row.get('All genes','')
    fmax = row.get('Feature maximum copy number','')
    print(f"- Classification: {cls}")
    print(f"  Feature ID: {fid}, AA amplicon #: {aa_num}, Sample: {sample}")
    print(f"  Captured interval length: {size}")
    if loc and pd.notna(loc):
        print(f"  Location: {loc}")
    if onc and pd.notna(onc):
        print(f"  Oncogenes: {onc}")
    if allg and pd.notna(allg):
        print(f"  All genes: {allg}")
    if fmax and pd.notna(fmax):
        print(f"  Feature max copy number: {fmax}")
    print("")

# Save outputs to variables as required
largest_per_class = largest_per_class_df
print("Variables saved: result, df, df_subset, largest_per_class_df (also largest_per_class)")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import json

print("Step: Querying CCLE amplicon table and computing largest amplicon per classification...")

# Query full table
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv')
print("Query result saved to variable 'result' (converted to DataFrame).")

# Convert to DataFrame
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
elif isinstance(result, list):
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)
elif isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result
else:
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)

print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
# Determine columns to use
# Prefer normalized lowercase columns if present, else use title-case ones
if 'classification' in df.columns:
    col_class = 'classification'
else:
    col_class = 'Classification'

if 'captured_interval_length' in df.columns:
    col_size = 'captured_interval_length'
else:
    col_size = 'Captured interval length'

meta_cols = []
for c in ['Sample name','sample_name','AA amplicon number','aa_amplicon_number','Feature ID','Oncogenes','All genes','Feature maximum copy number','feature_maximum_copy_number','Feature maximum copy number','Location','location','Feature maximum copy number']:
    if c in df.columns and c not in meta_cols:
        meta_cols.append(c)

print("Using classification column:", col_class)
print("Using size column:", col_size)
print("Including metadata columns (if present):", meta_cols)

# Build subset
cols_to_keep = [col_class, col_size] + meta_cols
df_subset = df.loc[:, [c for c in cols_to_keep if c in df.columns]].copy()

# Ensure numeric size
df_subset[col_size] = pd.to_numeric(df_subset[col_size], errors='coerce')

# Drop NA
df_subset = df_subset.dropna(subset=[col_class, col_size])
df_subset[col_class] = df_subset[col_class].astype(str).str.strip()

print("Rows with valid class and size:", df_subset.shape[0])

# Compute idxmax per classification
idx = df_subset.groupby(col_class)[col_size].idxmax()
largest_per_class_df = df_subset.loc[idx].reset_index(drop=True)

# Prepare concise output
summary_rows = []
for _, row in largest_per_class_df.iterrows():
    cls = row[col_class]
    size = int(row[col_size]) if pd.notna(row[col_size]) else None
    sample = row.get('Sample name') or row.get('sample_name') or ''
    aa_num = row.get('AA amplicon number') or row.get('aa_amplicon_number') or ''
    feature_max_cn = row.get('Feature maximum copy number') or row.get('feature_maximum_copy_number') or ''
    onc = row.get('Oncogenes','')
    loc = row.get('Location') or row.get('location') or ''
    # Trim location to first 200 chars for readability
    loc_trim = str(loc)
    if len(loc_trim) > 200:
        loc_trim = loc_trim[:200] + '...'
    # Convert oncogenes to a cleaned short string
    onc_str = str(onc)
    if len(onc_str) > 200:
        onc_str = onc_str[:200] + '...'
    summary_rows.append({
        'classification': cls,
        'captured_interval_length_bp': size,
        'sample': sample,
        'aa_amplicon_number': aa_num,
        'feature_max_copy_number': feature_max_cn,
        'oncogenes': onc_str,
        'location_trimmed': loc_trim
    })

# Save variable as required
largest_per_class = largest_per_class_df

print("\nLargest amplicon per classification (concise):")
print(json.dumps(summary_rows, indent=2))

# Also print a quick human-readable list
print("\nHuman-readable summary:")
for r in summary_rows:
    print(f"- {r['classification']}: {r['captured_interval_length_bp']:,} bp | Sample: {r['sample']} | AA#: {r['aa_amplicon_number']} | Max CN: {r['feature_max_copy_number']}")
    print(f"  Oncogenes: {r['oncogenes']}")
    print(f"  Location (trimmed): {r['location_trimmed']}")
    print("")

# Print completion marker
print("Computation complete. Variables available: result, df, df_subset, largest_per_class_df (alias largest_per_class).")

import pandas as pd

print("Step: Query amplicon table for tissue and complexity score columns")
result = query_amplicons(select=['Tissue of Origin','Complexity Score'], csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'")

print("\nInspecting top-level keys/summary of 'result':")
try:
    print("Result keys:", list(result.keys()))
except Exception as e:
    print("Could not list result keys:", e)

# Try to extract rows and convert to DataFrame

if rows is not None:
    print(f"Number of rows returned: {len(rows)}")
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
else:
    # fallback: try convert result to DataFrame directly
    print("No 'rows' key found; attempting to convert result directly to DataFrame")
    try:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Failed to convert result to DataFrame:", e)
        df = pd.DataFrame()

print("\nDataFrame columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# Determine which columns to use for tissue and complexity
tissue_col = None
comp_col = None
for c in df.columns:
    lc = c.lower()
    if tissue_col is None and 'tissue' in lc:
        tissue_col = c
    if comp_col is None and 'complex' in lc:
        comp_col = c

print("Detected columns -> tissue_col:", tissue_col, ", comp_col:", comp_col)

if tissue_col is None or comp_col is None:
    print("ERROR: Required columns not found. Aborting further computation.")
else:
    # Prepare data
    df_sub = df[[tissue_col, comp_col]].copy()
    df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=[tissue_col, comp_col])
    df_sub[comp_col] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub[comp_col], errors='coerce')
    df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=[comp_col])
    print(f"Rows after dropping NA: {len(df_sub)}")

    # Compute median complexity per tissue
    medians = df_sub.groupby(tissue_col)[comp_col].median().sort_values(ascending=False)
    print("\nMedian complexity per tissue (top 20):")
    print(medians.head(20))

    max_median = medians.max()
    top_tissues = medians[medians == max_median].index.tolist()
    print("\nMax median complexity value:", max_median)
    print("Tissue(s) with this max median complexity:", top_tissues)

    output = {'medians': medians.to_dict(), 'max_median': float(max_median), 'top_tissues': top_tissues}
    print("\nSaved output summary to variable 'output':")
    print(output)

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

print("Step: Query amplicon table for 'Tissue of origin' and 'Complexity score' columns")
result = query_amplicons(select=['Tissue of origin','Complexity score'], csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'")

# Print top-level info about result
print("\nResult type:", type(result))
if isinstance(result, dict):
    print("Result keys:", list(result.keys()))
else:
    print("Result content preview:", str(result)[:500])

# Extract rows and convert to DataFrame
rows = result.get('rows') if isinstance(result, dict) else None
if rows is None:
    raise RuntimeError("No 'rows' found in query result; cannot proceed.")
print(f"Number of rows returned: {len(rows)}")

df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# Identify columns for tissue and complexity
tissue_col = None
comp_col = None
for c in df.columns:
    lc = c.lower()
    if tissue_col is None and 'tissue' in lc:
        tissue_col = c
    if comp_col is None and 'complex' in lc:
        comp_col = c

print("Detected columns -> tissue_col:", tissue_col, ", comp_col:", comp_col)

# Prepare and clean data
df_sub = df[[tissue_col, comp_col]].copy()
initial_rows = len(df_sub)
df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=[tissue_col, comp_col])
after_dropna = len(df_sub)
df_sub[comp_col] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub[comp_col], errors='coerce')
df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=[comp_col])
final_rows = len(df_sub)
print(f"Rows: initial={initial_rows}, after dropping NA tissue/comp={after_dropna}, after coercion={final_rows}")

# Compute median complexity per tissue and counts
grouped = df_sub.groupby(tissue_col)[comp_col].agg(['median','count']).rename(columns={'median':'median_complexity','count':'n_amplicons'})
medians = grouped.sort_values(by='median_complexity', ascending=False)

print("\nTop tissues by median complexity (showing median and count):")
print(medians.head(20))

max_median = medians['median_complexity'].max()
top_tissues = medians[medians['median_complexity'] == max_median].index.tolist()
print("\nMax median complexity value:", max_median)
print("Tissue(s) with this max median complexity:", top_tissues)

# Save outputs
output = {'medians_df': medians, 'max_median': float(max_median), 'top_tissues': top_tissues}
print("\nSaved output summary to variable 'output'")
print("Top tissues and median complexity (as list):", list(zip(medians.index.tolist(), medians['median_complexity'].tolist()))[:10])

# Also print any tissues that tie for top with their counts
print("\nTissues tying for top median complexity with counts:")
for t in top_tissues:
    print(f"- {t}: median={max_median}, n_amplicons={int(medians.loc[t,'n_amplicons'])}")

# Keep variables for inspection
result_df = df
</execute>

<execute>
import ast
from pprint import pprint

print("Step: Parsing gene lists and building gene -> classifications mapping...")

rows = amp_df.get("rows", [])
print(f"Total rows to process: {len(rows)}")

def parse_gene_field(field):
    # Handle NaN-like and empty representations
    if field is None:
        return []
    # some entries are already lists? handle str mostly
    if isinstance(field, float):
        return []
    s = str(field).strip()
    if s in ["nan", "None", "['']", "['']"]:
        return []
    # Try literal_eval to get a list
    try:
        parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        if not isinstance(parsed, (list, tuple)):
            return []
        cleaned = []
        for item in parsed:
            if item is None:
                continue
            it = str(item).strip()
            # remove wrapping quotes if present (e.g., "'GENE'")
            if (it.startswith('"') and it.endswith('"')) or (it.startswith("'") and it.endswith("'")):
                it = it[1:-1]
            # strip any lingering quotes and whitespace
            it = it.strip().strip('"').strip("'").strip()
            if it:
                cleaned.append(it)
        return cleaned
    except Exception:
        # Fallback: try simple splits by comma
        parts = [p.strip().strip('"').strip("'") for p in s.strip("[]").split(",") if p.strip().strip('"').strip("'")]
        return parts

# Build mapping
gene_to_classes = {}
seen_classifications = set()
for r in rows:
    cls = r.get("classification") or r.get("Classification")
    if isinstance(cls, float):
        # nan
        continue
    if cls is None:
        continue
    cls = str(cls).strip()
    if not cls:
        continue
    seen_classifications.add(cls)
    all_genes_field = r.get("all_genes") or r.get("All genes") or ""
    oncogenes_field = r.get("oncogenes") or r.get("Oncogenes") or ""
    # parse both fields and combine (we consider any gene listed in either)
    genes_all = parse_gene_field(all_genes_field)
    genes_onc = parse_gene_field(oncogenes_field)
    genes = set(genes_all) | set(genes_onc)
    for g in genes:
        if g not in gene_to_classes:
            gene_to_classes[g] = set()
        gene_to_classes[g].add(cls)

print("Seen classifications in dataset:", seen_classifications)
print("Total unique genes observed:", len(gene_to_classes))

# Save mapping and print a small sample
print("\nSample gene -> classifications (first 10):")
sample_items = list(gene_to_classes.items())[:10]
pprint(sample_items)

# Save mapping for downstream use
result_mapping = gene_to_classes
print("Saved mapping to variable 'result_mapping'")

The user asked "Which chromosomes are most frequently amplified?" I'll analyze the CCLE amplicon dataset to count how often each chromosome appears in reported amplicons. Plan:

1. [ ] Import query_amplicons and load CCLE amplicon records (save output).

3. [ ] Parse genomic locations to count chromosome frequencies across amplicons and report the top chromosomes.

<execute>
# Step-by-step analysis of CCLE amplicon data to find most frequently amplified chromosomes
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import re
from collections import Counter
import pprint

print("1) Loading amplicon records from CCLE.csv using query_amplicons()...")

# Load all records from CCLE.csv (default dataset)
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', limit=None, add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print a short summary of the returned object
print("\nSaved query result to variable 'result'. Printing high-level keys/summary:")
if isinstance(result, dict):
    print("Result is a dict with keys:", list(result.keys()))
else:
    print("Result type:", type(result))

# Try to extract rows and schema information
rows = None
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    rows = result['rows']
elif isinstance(result, list):
    rows = result
else:
    # attempt common alternatives
    rows = result.get('data') if isinstance(result, dict) and 'data' in result else None

if rows is None:
    print("Unable to locate rows in result; printing top-level result for inspection:")
    pprint.pprint(result)
else:
    print(f"Number of amplicon records loaded: {len(rows)}")
    # Print available columns from first row
    first = rows[0]
    print("\nColumns/keys in first row (sample):")
    pprint.pprint(list(first.keys())[:50])
    print("\nSample row (first record):")
    pprint.pprint(first)

print("\n2) Identifying genomic location columns (searching for keys containing 'genom', 'loc', 'interval', or 'chr')...")
# Find candidate columns
candidate_cols = set()
if rows:
    for k in first.keys():
        kl = k.lower()
        if any(x in kl for x in ['genom', 'loc', 'interval', 'chr', 'segment']):
            candidate_cols.add(k)

print("Candidate genomic/location columns found:", candidate_cols)

print("\n3) Parsing genomic intervals from candidate columns and counting chromosome occurrences...")

chrom_counter = Counter()
total_amplicons = len(rows) if rows else 0

# regex to find chromosome labels like chr1, chr2, chrX, chrY, chrM
chrom_regex = re.compile(r'chr[0-9XYM]+', flags=re.IGNORECASE)

# For each amplicon record, collect unique chromosomes mentioned and count presence once per amplicon
for idx, r in enumerate(rows):
    chroms_in_record = set()
    for col in candidate_cols:
        val = r.get(col)
        if not val:
            continue
        # val might be list or string; convert to string
        s = val if isinstance(val, str) else str(val)
        found = chrom_regex.findall(s)
        for f in found:
            chroms_in_record.add(f.lower().replace('chr','chr'))  # normalize to lowercase 'chrN'
    # Also check other fields that might contain genomic intervals (scan all string fields as fallback)
    if not chroms_in_record:
        for k,v in r.items():
            if isinstance(v, str):
                found = chrom_regex.findall(v)
                for f in found:
                    chroms_in_record.add(f.lower().replace('chr','chr'))
    for c in chroms_in_record:
        chrom_counter[c] += 1

# Sort chromosomes by frequency
sorted_chroms = chrom_counter.most_common()

print(f"\nTotal amplicons processed: {total_amplicons}")
print("Chromosome counts (number of amplicons mentioning each chromosome):")
pprint.pprint(sorted_chroms)

# Print top 10 chromosomes
top_n = 10
print(f"\nTop {top_n} most frequently amplified chromosomes:")
for chrom, count in sorted_chroms[:top_n]:
    print(f"{chrom}: {count} amplicons ({count/total_amplicons:.3%} of amplicons)")

# Save outputs for inspection
analysis_output = {
    'total_amplicons': total_amplicons,
    'chrom_counts': chrom_counter,
    'sorted_chroms': sorted_chroms
}

print("\nSaved detailed analysis to variable 'analysis_output'.")
print("Done.")

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
import math
import re
from collections import defaultdict

print("Step: Querying CCLE amplicon table for sample_name and oncogenes...")

# Query the data (all rows), requesting normalized column names
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', select=['sample_name','oncogenes'])
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'. Summary:")
print(result.get('summary', 'No summary'))
print("Total rows reported:", result.get('row_count_total', 'unknown'))
print("Rows returned in object:", result.get('row_count_returned', len(result.get('rows', []))))

rows = result.get('rows', [])
print(f"Processing {len(rows)} rows...")

# Parse oncogenes and count unique samples per gene
gene_to_samples = defaultdict(set)
rows_processed = 0
rows_with_oncogenes = 0

def clean_gene_token(tok):
    # remove surrounding quotes and whitespace
    if tok is None:
        return ''
    s = str(tok).strip()
    # strip common wrapping quotes
    s = s.strip('"').strip("'").strip()
    return s

for r in rows:
    rows_processed += 1
    sample = r.get('sample_name')
    onc_val = r.get('oncogenes')
    if onc_val is None:
        continue
    # Some entries may be NaN floats
    if isinstance(onc_val, float) and math.isnan(onc_val):
        continue
    # If it's already a list, use it; otherwise try to literal_eval string
    parsed = None
    if isinstance(onc_val, list):
        parsed = onc_val
    else:
        s = str(onc_val)
        # handle common patterns like "['']" or '["\'E2F3\'", "\'SOX4\'"]'
        try:
            parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        except Exception:
            # fallback: attempt to extract tokens between quotes
            parsed = re.findall(r"'([^']*)'|\"([^\"]*)\"", s)
            # flatten capture groups
            flat = []
            for a,b in parsed:
                flat.append(a if a else b)
            parsed = flat

    # normalize parsed to list of tokens
    if not isinstance(parsed, (list, tuple)):
        continue
    cleaned = []
    for tok in parsed:
        token = clean_gene_token(tok)
        if token == '':
            continue
        cleaned.append(token)
    if len(cleaned) == 0:
        continue
    rows_with_oncogenes += 1
    for gene in cleaned:
        gene_to_samples[gene].add(sample)

print(f"Rows processed: {rows_processed}")
print(f"Rows with >=1 oncogene token: {rows_with_oncogenes}")
print(f"Unique genes found: {len(gene_to_samples)}")

# Compute counts per gene (unique samples)
gene_counts = {g: len(samps) for g, samps in gene_to_samples.items()}
# Sort descending by count, then alphabetically
sorted_genes = sorted(gene_counts.items(), key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0]))

# Save outputs as required
result_counts = sorted_genes
print("\nTop 30 oncogenes by number of unique samples (gene : unique_sample_count):")
for g, c in result_counts[:30]:
    print(f"{g} : {c}")

# Print a small sample of gene->sample sets for verification
sample_check = list(gene_to_samples.items())[:5]
print("\nSample of gene->sample sets (first 5):")
for gene, samps in sample_check:
    print(gene, "->", list(samps)[:5])

# Save outputs to variables (explicit)
saved_gene_to_samples = gene_to_samples
saved_gene_counts = gene_counts

print("\nDone. Variables saved: result, result_counts, saved_gene_to_samples, saved_gene_counts")
</execute>

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
import math
import re
from collections import defaultdict

# Modified Step 2: Query all rows (no select) and inspect
print("Querying CCLE amplicon table (all columns)...")
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv')
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'. Summary:")
print(result.get('summary', 'No summary'))
print("Total rows reported:", result.get('row_count_total', 'unknown'))
print("Rows returned in object:", result.get('row_count_returned', len(result.get('rows', []))))

rows = result.get('rows', [])
if len(rows) == 0:
    print("No rows returned; exiting.")
else:
    print("\nKeys in first row:")
    first_keys = list(rows[0].keys())
    print(first_keys)

# Steps 3-4: Parse oncogenes and count unique samples per gene
gene_to_samples = defaultdict(set)

def clean_gene_token(tok):
    if tok is None:
        return ''
    s = str(tok).strip()
    # strip surrounding quotes if present
    s = s.strip().strip('"').strip("'").strip()
    return s

rows_processed = 0
rows_with_oncogenes = 0

for r in rows:
    rows_processed += 1
    # Prefer original column names as present in file
    sample = r.get('Sample name') or r.get('sample_name') or r.get('Sample') or None
    onc_val = r.get('Oncogenes') or r.get('oncogenes')
    if sample is None:
        # skip if no sample identifier
        continue
    if onc_val is None:
        continue
    # Handle NaN
    if isinstance(onc_val, float) and math.isnan(onc_val):
        continue
    parsed = None
    # If it's already a list/tuple
    if isinstance(onc_val, (list, tuple)):
        parsed = onc_val
    else:
        s = str(onc_val)
        # Try literal_eval first
        try:
            parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        except Exception:
            # Fallback: extract tokens inside single or double quotes
            tokens = re.findall(r"'([^']*)'|\"([^\"]*)\"", s)
            flat = []
            for a, b in tokens:
                flat.append(a if a else b)
            parsed = flat

    if not isinstance(parsed, (list, tuple)):
        continue
    # Clean tokens
    cleaned = []
    for tok in parsed:
        token = clean_gene_token(tok)
        if token == '' or token.lower() == 'nan':
            continue
        cleaned.append(token)
    if len(cleaned) == 0:
        continue
    rows_with_oncogenes += 1
    for gene in cleaned:
        gene_to_samples[gene].add(sample)

print(f"\nRows processed: {rows_processed}")
print(f"Rows with >=1 oncogene token: {rows_with_oncogenes}")
print(f"Unique genes found: {len(gene_to_samples)}")

# Compute counts per gene (unique samples)
gene_counts = {g: len(samps) for g, samps in gene_to_samples.items()}
sorted_genes = sorted(gene_counts.items(), key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0]))

# Print top 50 oncogenes by unique sample count
top_n = 50
print(f"\nTop {top_n} oncogenes by number of unique samples (gene : unique_sample_count):")
for g, c in sorted_genes[:top_n]:
    print(f"{g} : {c}")

# Print small verification: a gene -> sample list snippet for top 5 genes
print("\nVerification: sample lists for top 5 genes:")
for g, c in sorted_genes[:5]:
    samps = list(gene_to_samples[g])
    print(f"{g} ({c} samples) -> {samps[:10]}{'...' if len(samps)>10 else ''}")

# Save variables as required
saved_result = result
saved_gene_to_samples = gene_to_samples
saved_gene_counts = gene_counts
saved_sorted_genes = sorted_genes

print("\nDone. Variables saved: saved_result, saved_gene_to_samples, saved_gene_counts, saved_sorted_genes")
</execute>

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
from collections import defaultdict, Counter

# Step 1: Query the CCLE amplicon table (no select to avoid column name mismatch)
print("Querying CCLE.csv (this may return all rows)...")
result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save the query output as required
saved_query_result = result
print("Saved query result into variable 'saved_query_result'.")

# Inspect top-level info
total_rows = result.get('row_count_total', None)
returned = result.get('row_count_returned', None)
print(f"Row counts: total={total_rows}, returned={returned}")

rows = result.get('rows', [])
print(f"Number of rows in 'rows': {len(rows)}")

# Step 2: Parse oncogene strings and build mapping tissue -> gene -> set(samples)
tissue_gene_samples = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(set))
parse_problems = 0

for i, r in enumerate(rows):
    # robustly access fields (normalized and original names)
    sample = r.get('sample_name') or r.get('Sample name')
    tissue = r.get('tissue_of_origin') or r.get('Tissue of origin')
    onc_raw = r.get('oncogenes') or r.get('Oncogenes')
    # skip rows lacking tissue or sample
    if not sample or not tissue:
        parse_problems += 1
        continue
    # onc_raw expected to be a string representation of a list, e.g. '["\'E2F3\'", "\'SOX4\'"]' or "['']"
    genes = []
    try:
        if onc_raw is None:
            genes = []
        else:
            # If already a list (some tooling might return list), handle that
            if isinstance(onc_raw, list):
                raw_list = onc_raw
            else:
                # Try to literal_eval; if it's "['']" this becomes ['']
                raw_list = ast.literal_eval(onc_raw)
            # normalize each entry by stripping extra quotes and whitespace
            for item in raw_list:
                if item is None:
                    continue
                if not isinstance(item, str):
                    item = str(item)
                # strip surrounding whitespace and both types of quotes
                gene = item.strip().strip('"').strip("'").strip()
                if gene == "" or gene.lower() == "''":
                    continue
                genes.append(gene)
    except Exception as e:
        parse_problems += 1
        # try a fallback: if onc_raw looks like "['GENE1','GENE2']" split by commas
        try:
            txt = str(onc_raw)
            txt = txt.strip().lstrip('[').rstrip(']')
            parts = [p.strip().strip('"').strip("'") for p in txt.split(',') if p.strip()]
            genes = [p for p in parts if p]
        except:
            genes = []
    # For each gene, add this sample to the set for this tissue
    for g in genes:
        tissue_gene_samples[tissue][g].add(sample)

# Summary:
num_tissues = len(tissue_gene_samples)
total_genes = sum(len(d) for d in tissue_gene_samples.values())
print(f"Processed {len(rows)} rows. Number of tissues found: {num_tissues}")
print(f"Total distinct tissue-gene pairs: {total_genes}")
print(f"Number of problematic/ skipped parse rows: {parse_problems}")

# Step 3: Compute top 5 genes per tissue by unique sample count
final_results = {}
for tissue, gene_map in tissue_gene_samples.items():
    # create list of (gene, count)
    gene_counts = [(gene, len(samples)) for gene, samples in gene_map.items()]
    # sort by count desc, then gene name asc for tie-break
    gene_counts.sort(key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0]))
    top5 = gene_counts[:5]
    final_results[tissue] = top5

# Save final_results variable as required
saved_final_results = final_results
print("Saved final results into variable 'saved_final_results'.")

# Step 4: Present results (print them). Also show a compact sample for first 10 tissues.
print("\n=== Top 5 amplified oncogenes per tissue (by unique samples) ===\n")
sorted_tissues = sorted(final_results.keys())
for tissue in sorted_tissues:
    top5 = final_results[tissue]
    print(f"Tissue: {tissue}")
    if not top5:
        print("  (no oncogenes found)")
    else:
        for rank, (gene, cnt) in enumerate(top5, start=1):
            print(f"  {rank}. {gene}  (unique samples: {cnt})")
    print("-----")

# Print a small sample (first 10 tissues) again in condensed form
print("\nSample condensed output for first 10 tissues:")
for tissue in sorted_tissues[:10]:
    top5 = final_results[tissue]
    entries = [f"{g} ({c})" for g, c in top5]
    print(f"{tissue}: " + ", ".join(entries))

# Also print counts of tissues and some examples
print(f"\nTotal tissues: {num_tissues}")
print("Example tissue keys:", sorted_tissues[:8])
</execute>

================================ Human Message =================================

Which tissues have the highest fraction of BFB features?
================================== Ai Message ==================================

Thinking and reasoning:
I will query the CCLE amplicon data to compute, for each tissue of origin, the fraction of amplicons classified as BFB (breakage-fusion-bridge). <execute>from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("Step 2: Querying the CCLE amplicon dataset...")

# Query the CCLE dataset
all_res = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved query result to variable all_res. Type:", type(all_res))

# Convert the returned object to a DataFrame for processing (handle dict or DataFrame)
if isinstance(all_res, dict):
    # The tool may return rows in different keys; try common possibilities
    if 'rows' in all_res:
        df = pd.DataFrame(all_res['rows'])
    elif 'data' in all_res:
        df = pd.DataFrame(all_res['data'])
    else:
        # fallback to DataFrame of the dict
        df = pd.DataFrame([all_res])
else:
    # assume it's already a DataFrame-like or list of dicts
    df = pd.DataFrame(all_res)

print("\nStep 2 result: dataframe created.")
print("Number of rows:", len(df))
print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# Inspect first few rows for context
print("\nFirst 5 rows:")
print(df.head().to_string())

# Step 3: Identify tissue and classification columns
# try normalized column names first
possible_tissue_cols = [c for c in df.columns if 'tissue' in c.lower()]
possible_class_cols = [c for c in df.columns if 'class' in c.lower() or 'classification' in c.lower()]

print("\nDetected potential tissue columns:", possible_tissue_cols)
print("Detected potential classification columns:", possible_class_cols)

# Choose best matches
if possible_tissue_cols:
    tissue_col = possible_tissue_cols[0]
else:
    raise ValueError("Could not find a tissue-like column in the dataset.")

if possible_class_cols:
    class_col = possible_class_cols[0]
else:
    raise ValueError("Could not find a classification-like column in the dataset.")

print(f"\nUsing tissue column: '{tissue_col}' and classification column: '{class_col}'")

# Normalize classification text
df[class_col] = df[class_col].astype(str).str.strip()

# Compute per-tissue totals and BFB counts
totals = df.groupby(tissue_col).size().rename('total_count')
bfb = df[df[class_col].str.upper() == 'BFB'].groupby(tissue_col).size().rename('bfb_count')

summary = pd.concat([totals, bfb], axis=1).fillna(0)
summary['bfb_count'] = summary['bfb_count'].astype(int)
summary['bfb_fraction'] = summary['bfb_count'] / summary['total_count']
summary = summary.sort_values(by='bfb_fraction', ascending=False)

print("\nStep 3 result: per-tissue BFB summary (top 20 by fraction):")
print(summary.head(20).to_string())

# Save result for reporting
result_df = summary.reset_index().rename(columns={tissue_col: 'tissue'})

print("\nSaved result_df. Preview:")
print(result_df.head(10).to_string())

# Print full top results (top tissues with non-zero BFB fraction)
top_nonzero = result_df[result_df['bfb_count']>0].sort_values('bfb_fraction', ascending=False)
print("\nTop tissues with non-zero BFB fraction (sorted):")
print(top_nonzero.head(50).to_string())

# Save outputs in variables (to satisfy "save the output and print")
query_result = all_res
summary_table = result_df

print("\nStep 4: Completed computation and saved outputs to query_result and summary_table variables.")</execute>

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import re
import itertools
from collections import Counter

print("Step: Re-querying ecDNA amplicons and processing oncogenes per sample...")

# 1) Query ecDNA amplicons (save and print result summary)
res = query_amplicons(classification='ecDNA', csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved res from query_amplicons. row_count_returned:", res.get('row_count_returned'))

# 2) Convert to DataFrame
df = pd.DataFrame(res['rows'])
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# 3) Parse oncogenes column into list of gene symbols
def parse_oncogenes_field(x):
    if pd.isna(x):
        return []
    s = str(x)
    # Extract gene-like tokens (letters, digits, underscore, hyphen)
    toks = re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9_\-]+", s)
    # Remove duplicates while preserving order
    seen = set()
    out = []
    for t in toks:
        if t.upper() not in seen:
            seen.add(t.upper())
            out.append(t.upper())
    return out

df['oncogene_list'] = df['oncogenes'].apply(parse_oncogenes_field)

# Show examples
print("\nExample parsed oncogene lists (first 10 rows):")
for i, row in df.head(10).iterrows():
    print(i, row['sample_name'], "->", row['oncogene_list'])

# 4) Aggregate oncogenes per sample (union across ecDNA amplicons in that sample)
sample_oncogene_sets = df.groupby('sample_name')['oncogene_list'].apply(
    lambda lists: set().union(*[set(l) for l in lists if l])
)
sample_oncogene_sets = sample_oncogene_sets.to_dict()

total_samples_with_ecDNA = len(sample_oncogene_sets)
print("\nTotal unique samples with ecDNA:", total_samples_with_ecDNA)

# 5) For each sample, generate unordered oncogene pairs (combinations) and count them across samples
pair_counter = Counter()
for sample, geneset in sample_oncogene_sets.items():
    genes = sorted([g for g in geneset if g])  # sorted list
    # generate unique unordered pairs per sample (each pair counted at most once per sample)
    for a,b in itertools.combinations(genes, 2):
        pair = tuple(sorted((a,b)))
        pair_counter[pair] += 1

# 6) Prepare top pairs (most common)
top_n = 30
most_common_pairs = pair_counter.most_common(top_n)

# Print top results
print(f"\nTop {top_n} oncogene pairs (count of samples with both genes on ecDNA, fraction of samples):")
print("Rank\tPair\tCount\tFraction")
for idx, (pair, cnt) in enumerate(most_common_pairs, start=1):
    frac = cnt / total_samples_with_ecDNA
    print(f"{idx}\t{pair[0]}--{pair[1]}\t{cnt}\t{frac:.3f}")

# Save outputs for downstream use/inspection
result_summary = {
    "total_samples_with_ecDNA": total_samples_with_ecDNA,
    "pair_counts": pair_counter,
    "top_pairs": most_common_pairs
}
print("\nSaved result_summary.")
result_summary
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast
import math
from collections import defaultdict

# Re-use data_result if present; otherwise load
try:
    data_result
    print("Using previously loaded data_result.")
except NameError:
    print("Loading CCLE amplicon table...")
    data_result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", limit=None, add_normalized_columns=True)

rows = data_result['rows']
print("Total rows (amplicons):", len(rows))

# Step 2: inspect keys/columns from the schema / first row
first_row = rows[0]
print("Columns in row (sample):", list(first_row.keys())[:60])

# Identify likely column names for sample and location
# Common names observed in preview: 'Sample name', 'Location', 'AA amplicon number'
sample_col = None
location_col = None
amp_num_col = None
for k in first_row.keys():
    kl = k.lower()
    if 'sample' in kl and sample_col is None:
        sample_col = k
    if kl in ('location', 'locations', 'location(s)') and location_col is None:
        location_col = k
    if 'aa amplicon' in kl or 'amplicon' in kl and amp_num_col is None:
        amp_num_col = k

print("Detected columns -> sample_col:", sample_col, "; location_col:", location_col, "; amp_num_col:", amp_num_col)

# Helper to parse a single Location field into a list of interval strings
def parse_location_field(loc):
    # Handle NaN / empty
    if loc is None:
        return []
    if isinstance(loc, float) and math.isnan(loc):
        return []
    if isinstance(loc, (list, tuple)):
        return list(loc)
    # loc is likely a string representation: e.g., "['chr6:20350615-22839372']" or "['']"
    s = str(loc).strip()
    if s == "['']" or s == "[]":
        return []
    try:
        parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        # parsed might be list of strings or nested quoting; coerce to strings and filter empties
        parsed = [p for p in [str(x).strip().strip('"').strip("'") for x in parsed] if p and p != "''"]
        return parsed
    except Exception:
        # fallback: try to split on commas
        parts = [p.strip().strip('"').strip("'") for p in s.strip("[]").split(",") if p.strip()]
        parts = [p for p in parts if p and p != "''"]
        return parts

# Function to extract chromosome label from an interval like 'chr6:20350615-22839372'
def chrom_from_interval(interval):
    if not interval:
        return None
    if ':' in interval:
        chrom = interval.split(':',1)[0].strip()
        return chrom
    # sometimes entries might just be 'chr6' or similar
    if interval.startswith('chr'):
        return interval.strip()
    return None

# Step 3: iterate rows and compute chromosome sets
per_amp_chroms = []  # list of dicts with sample, amp_num, chroms, row_index
sample_to_chroms = defaultdict(set)
sample_to_amp_multichr = defaultdict(list)  # list of amplicon records that are multi-chr

for i, r in enumerate(rows):
    sample = r.get(sample_col, None)
    amp_num = r.get(amp_num_col, None)
    loc_raw = r.get(location_col, None)
    intervals = parse_location_field(loc_raw)
    chroms = set()
    for itv in intervals:
        c = chrom_from_interval(itv)
        if c:
            chroms.add(c)
    per_amp_chroms.append({'row_index': i, 'sample': sample, 'amp_num': amp_num, 'intervals': intervals, 'chroms': chroms})
    if sample and chroms:
        sample_to_chroms[sample].update(chroms)
    # record multi-chromosome amplicon
    if len(chroms) > 1:
        sample_to_amp_multichr[sample].append({'row_index': i, 'amp_num': amp_num, 'chroms': sorted(chroms), 'intervals': intervals})

# Save outputs
result_per_amp = per_amp_chroms
result_sample_chroms = dict(sample_to_chroms)
result_sample_amp_multi = dict(sample_to_amp_multichr)

# Print summary stats
num_amp_multi = sum(1 for a in per_amp_chroms if len(a['chroms'])>1)
num_samples_with_multi_amp = len([s for s,ch in result_sample_chroms.items() if len(ch)>1])
num_samples_with_amp_multi_per_amp = len([s for s in result_sample_amp_multi.keys()])

print("Amplicons spanning multiple chromosomes (per-amplicon multi-chr):", num_amp_multi)
print("Samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (across amplicons):", num_samples_with_multi_amp)
print("Samples that have at least one single amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes:", num_samples_with_amp_multi_per_amp)

# Print some examples (first 10) for each category
examples_amp_multi = [a for a in per_amp_chroms if len(a['chroms'])>1][:10]
examples_sample_multi = [s for s,ch in result_sample_chroms.items() if len(ch)>1][:10]
examples_sample_amp_multi = list(result_sample_amp_multi.items())[:10]

print("\nExamples of per-amplicon multi-chr amplicons (up to 10):")
for a in examples_amp_multi:
    print(f"row {a['row_index']}, sample {a['sample']}, amp_num {a['amp_num']}, chroms {sorted(a['chroms'])}, intervals {a['intervals']}")

print("\nExamples of samples with amplification across multiple chromosomes (up to 10):")
for s in examples_sample_multi:
    print(f"{s} -> chromosomes: {sorted(result_sample_chroms[s])}")

print("\nExamples of samples with at least one amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes (up to 10):")
for s, recs in examples_sample_amp_multi:
    print(f"{s} -> {len(recs)} multi-chr amplicon(s); example chroms: {recs[0]['chroms']}")

# Save results variables (must save outputs and print)
saved_results = {
    'per_amp_chroms': result_per_amp,
    'sample_to_chroms': result_sample_chroms,
    'sample_to_amp_multichr': result_sample_amp_multi
}
print("\nSaved results keys:", list(saved_results.keys()))

# Print completion
print("Step 2 and 3 complete: parsed locations and computed chromosome sets.")
</execute><observation>Count of samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (overall): 102

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import math
import ast
from collections import defaultdict

# Reuse previously computed saved_results if available; otherwise recompute quickly
try:
    saved_results
    print("Using previously saved_results.")
    sample_to_chroms = saved_results['sample_to_chroms']
    sample_to_amp_multi = saved_results['sample_to_amp_multichr']
except NameError:
    # Minimal recomputation as fallback
    data_result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", limit=None, add_normalized_columns=True)
    rows = data_result['rows']
    # helpers
    def parse_location_field(loc):
        if loc is None:
            return []
        if isinstance(loc, float) and math.isnan(loc):
            return []
        try:
            parsed = ast.literal_eval(str(loc))
            parsed = [p for p in [str(x).strip().strip('"').strip("'") for x in parsed] if p and p != "''"]
            return parsed
        except Exception:
            s = str(loc)
            parts = [p.strip().strip('"').strip("'") for p in s.strip("[]").split(",") if p.strip()]
            parts = [p for p in parts if p and p != "''"]
            return parts
    def chrom_from_interval(interval):
        if not interval:
            return None
        if ':' in interval:
            return interval.split(':',1)[0].strip()
        if interval.startswith('chr'):
            return interval.strip()
        return None

    sample_to_chroms = defaultdict(set)
    sample_to_amp_multi = defaultdict(list)
    for i, r in enumerate(rows):
        sample = r.get('Sample name', None)
        loc_raw = r.get('Location', None)
        intervals = parse_location_field(loc_raw)
        chroms = set()
        for itv in intervals:
            c = chrom_from_interval(itv)
            if c:
                chroms.add(c)
        if sample and chroms:
            sample_to_chroms[sample].update(chroms)
        if len(chroms) > 1:
            sample_to_amp_multi[sample].append({'row_index': i, 'chroms': sorted(chroms), 'intervals': intervals})

# Build final lists
samples_multi_chr_overall = sorted([s for s, ch in sample_to_chroms.items() if len(ch) > 1])
samples_with_amp_spanning_multi = sorted(list(sample_to_amp_multi.keys()))

# Print counts
print("Count: samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (overall, sample-level):", len(samples_multi_chr_overall))
print("Count: samples with at least one single amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes (per-amplicon):", len(samples_with_amp_spanning_multi))
print("")

# Print the full lists (sample -> sorted chromosome list) for overall multi-chromosome samples
print("Samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (sample -> chromosomes):")
for s in samples_multi_chr_overall:
    chroms = sorted(list(sample_to_chroms[s]))
    print(f"{s} -> {chroms}")
print("")
# Print the per-amplicon multi-chromosome samples with number of such amplicons and example chrom sets
print("Samples with at least one amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes (sample -> number of multi-chr amplicons; example chrom sets):")
for s in samples_with_amp_spanning_multi:
    recs = sample_to_amp_multi[s]
    chrom_sets = [r['chroms'] for r in recs]
    print(f"{s} -> {len(recs)} amplicon(s); chrom sets example(s): {chrom_sets[:3]}")
    
# Save outputs for possible further use
final_results = {
    'samples_multi_chr_overall': samples_multi_chr_overall,
    'samples_with_amp_spanning_multi': samples_with_amp_spanning_multi,
    'sample_to_chroms': dict(sample_to_chroms),
    'sample_to_amp_multi': dict(sample_to_amp_multi)
}
print("\nStep 4 complete: lists produced and saved in final_results.")
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast, math
from collections import defaultdict

print("Loading CCLE amplicon table and parsing locations...")
data = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", limit=None, add_normalized_columns=True)
rows = data['rows']

def parse_location_field(loc):
    if loc is None:
        return []
    if isinstance(loc, float) and math.isnan(loc):
        return []
    s = str(loc)
    # try literal_eval for list-like strings
    try:
        parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        parsed = [str(x).strip().strip('"').strip("'") for x in parsed]
        parsed = [p for p in parsed if p and p != "''"]
        return parsed
    except Exception:
        # fallback split
        parts = [p.strip().strip('"').strip("'") for p in s.strip("[]").split(",") if p.strip()]
        parts = [p for p in parts if p and p != "''"]
        return parts

def chrom_from_interval(interval):
    if not interval:
        return None
    if ':' in interval:
        return interval.split(':',1)[0].strip()
    if interval.startswith('chr'):
        return interval.strip()
    return None

sample_col = 'Sample name'
location_col = 'Location'
amp_col = 'AA amplicon number'

sample_to_chroms = defaultdict(set)
sample_to_amp_multi = defaultdict(list)

for i, r in enumerate(rows):
    sample = r.get(sample_col)
    loc_raw = r.get(location_col)
    intervals = parse_location_field(loc_raw)
    chroms = set()
    for itv in intervals:
        c = chrom_from_interval(itv)
        if c:
            chroms.add(c)
    if sample and chroms:
        sample_to_chroms[sample].update(chroms)
    if len(chroms) > 1:
        sample_to_amp_multi[sample].append({'row_index': i, 'amp_num': r.get(amp_col), 'chroms': sorted(chroms), 'intervals': intervals})

# Build final lists
samples_multi_chr_overall = sorted([s for s, ch in sample_to_chroms.items() if len(ch) > 1])
samples_with_amp_spanning_multi = sorted(list(sample_to_amp_multi.keys()))

# Print counts and full lists
print(f"\nCount: samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (sample-level): {len(samples_multi_chr_overall)}")
print(f"Count: samples with at least one amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes (per-amplicon): {len(samples_with_amp_spanning_multi)}\n")

print("Samples with amplifications across multiple chromosomes (sample -> chromosomes):")
for s in samples_multi_chr_overall:
    chroms = sorted(list(sample_to_chroms[s]))
    print(f"{s} -> {chroms}")

print("\nSamples that have at least one single amplicon spanning multiple chromosomes (sample -> number of such amplicons):")
for s in samples_with_amp_spanning_multi:
    print(f"{s} -> {len(sample_to_amp_multi[s])} amplicon(s); chrom sets example(s): {[r['chroms'] for r in sample_to_amp_multi[s][:3]]}")

# Save for downstream use
final_results = {
    'samples_multi_chr_overall': samples_multi_chr_overall,
    'samples_with_amp_spanning_multi': samples_with_amp_spanning_multi,
    'sample_to_chroms': dict(sample_to_chroms),
    'sample_to_amp_multi': dict(sample_to_amp_multi)
}

print("\nStep 4 and 5 complete: results saved in final_results.")

<execute> 
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import ast
import numpy as np

print("Step: Querying CCLE.csv for Feature ID, Classification, and Oncogenes...")

# Query the amplicon table (save output as required)
result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv",
                        select=["Feature ID","Classification","Oncogenes"],
                        add_normalized_columns=True)

print("Query result type:", type(result))
# Print a small preview of the returned object
try:
    # If it's a DataFrame-like
    df = result.copy()
    print("Returned DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
    print("Returned DataFrame head:")
    print(df.head(10).to_string(index=False))
except Exception as e:
    print("Could not treat result as DataFrame directly:", e)
    # Try to coerce to DataFrame
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)
    print("Coerced DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
    print(df.head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Normalize column names if needed
cols = df.columns.tolist()
# find likely column names
feature_col = None
class_col = None
onc_col = None
for c in cols:
    low = c.lower()
    if low in ("feature id","feature_id","featureid"):
        feature_col = c
    if low in ("classification","classification"):
        class_col = c
    if low in ("oncogenes","oncogene","oncogenes"):
        onc_col = c

print(f"Detected columns -> feature: {feature_col}, classification: {class_col}, oncogenes: {onc_col}")

# Ensure we have the required columns
if not (feature_col and class_col and onc_col):
    raise ValueError("Required columns not found in query result.")

# Prepare dataframe for processing
proc_df = df[[feature_col, class_col, onc_col]].rename(columns={feature_col: "feature_id", class_col: "classification", onc_col: "oncogenes_raw"})

print("\nProcessing oncogenes field to compute counts per feature...")

def parse_oncogenes_field(x):
    # Handle NaN
    if pd.isna(x):
        return []
    # If it's already a list
    if isinstance(x, (list, tuple)):
        lst = list(x)
    else:
        # Try ast.literal_eval for strings like "['E2F3', 'SOX4']"
        s = str(x).strip()
        try:
            lst = ast.literal_eval(s)
            # If literal_eval returns a single string (not list), make it a list
            if isinstance(lst, str):
                lst = [lst]
        except Exception:
            # Fallback: strip brackets and quotes then split by comma
            s2 = s.strip("[]")
            if s2 == "":
                lst = []
            else:
                parts = [p.strip().strip("\"'") for p in s2.split(",")]
                lst = parts
    # Count only non-empty names
    clean = [str(i).strip() for i in lst if str(i).strip() != ""]
    return clean

# Apply parsing and compute oncogene counts (count of non-empty genes)
proc_df["oncogene_list"] = proc_df["oncogenes_raw"].apply(parse_oncogenes_field)
proc_df["oncogene_count"] = proc_df["oncogene_list"].apply(len)

print("Sample processed rows (first 10):")
print(proc_df.head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Group by classification and compute distribution
grouped = proc_df.groupby("classification")

distributions = {}
summaries = {}

for name, group in grouped:
    counts = group["oncogene_count"].value_counts().sort_index()
    # Convert to dictionary {count: number_of_features}
    dist_dict = {int(k): int(v) for k, v in counts.items()}
    distributions[name] = dist_dict
    # Summary stats
    summaries[name] = {
        "n_features": int(len(group)),
        "mean_oncogenes": float(group["oncogene_count"].mean()),
        "median_oncogenes": float(group["oncogene_count"].median()),
        "std_oncogenes": float(group["oncogene_count"].std(ddof=0)) if len(group) > 1 else 0.0,
        "min_oncogenes": int(group["oncogene_count"].min()),
        "max_oncogenes": int(group["oncogene_count"].max())
    }

print("\nComputed distributions and summaries for each classification (saved in variables).")

# Print distributions and summaries in readable form
for cls in sorted(distributions.keys()):
    print("\nClassification:", cls)
    print("Number of features:", summaries[cls]["n_features"])
    print("Oncogene count distribution (oncogenes_per_feature: number_of_features):")
    for cnt, num in sorted(distributions[cls].items()):
        print(f"  {cnt}: {num}")
    print("Summary stats:", {k: summaries[cls][k] for k in ["mean_oncogenes","median_oncogenes","std_oncogenes","min_oncogenes","max_oncogenes"]})

# Save outputs to variables for inspection if needed
query_result = result
distribution_result = distributions
summary_result = summaries

print("\nFinished.")

# Print brief confirmation that outputs are saved
print("Saved variables: query_result, distribution_result, summary_result")
</execute>
